The ticket asked for a decision before building; both halves were needed and both shipped. (a) `ProjectCard.available` is a filesystem fact computed per read — when the directory is gone `toCard` doesn't even probe the DB, since the stats would be zeros either way and zeros alone are indistinguishable from a real empty project (the honest-absence rule `healthz` and the trace view already follow). The card renders a distinct dashed **Unavailable** state naming the missing path, with no Open/Archive and no counters at all rather than zeroed ones. (b) `removeProject` + `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id` (204) forget the registry entry. **The sharp edge is structural, not merely tested:** `removeProject` has no filesystem write path other than `saveRegistry`, so it cannot delete a repo or its `.dokima/state.db`.

**Three mutations, three reds — the gates are real, not read.** (1) `available = true` → the vanished-directory test reds alone, 581 others stay green. (2) `removeProject` also `fs.rm`s the project dir → both the unit sharp-edge test AND the route test red, each asserting against the filesystem rather than the API response. (3) `if (!card.available)` → `if (false)` in the card → the e2e reds on the missing `data-unavailable` attribute.

**The feature exposed a latent test defect.** `app.spec.ts`'s `getByText('Dokima')` matched case-insensitively and substring-wise, so once an unavailable card began rendering its `/tmp/dokima-*` path it hit 4 elements and violated strict mode. That assertion was always one stale registry entry from failing; it is now scoped to `.app-shell__header`. Also worth noting: `pnpm test` stayed green through a real type error — two `ProjectCard` fixtures were missing the new required `available` field, and only `tsc` caught it. Law 3 runs both for exactly this reason.

**SCOPE NOTE (schema defect, not a workaround).** W9-15's declared `write_scope` is `apps/web/src/fleet/**`, `apps/server/src/api/projects.ts`, `apps/server/src/api/server/**` — which does not cover the tests its own acceptance criteria mandate. Writing them required `apps/server/src/api/projects.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/fleet.spec.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/app.spec.ts` and `apps/web/src/onboarding/FirstRunWizard.test.tsx` (the last two forced by the change, not chosen). This is precisely the upstream class captured as W10-07 in `docs/work/W10_PLAN.md` — attest v3.1.9–11's `TEST_SIBLING_STRICT`, "a write_scope must cover its own tests". Recorded rather than silently widened.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2888 passed | 3 skipped** (+5), **59 e2e passed** (+1).

2026-08-02 W9-08 done — the phase gate can pass again. Blocked since 2026-07-28, and **the block was never about the code**: the fix had been complete and green on `sw/w9-08` for five days, unable to merge because editing a byte of `content/validators/validate-mermaid.sh` invalidates its hash in the signed pack, and re-signing needs a private key nobody had. The 2026-08-02 key rotation (forced by the leaked-key incident) produced that key as a side effect, so this ticket unblocked itself. Cherry-picked `4df70ad` onto the post-rename main — clean, all four in-scope files, no stale `SHIPWRIGHT_*` in the picked content — then re-signed.

**Two defects, both reporting bugs, neither touching a validator's judgement (AC5).** Defect 1: `validate-mermaid.sh` was the only validator that never sourced `_lib.sh`, so a clean scan exited 0 with zero-byte stdout; `parseValidatorOutput('')` correctly returns null and `run.ts` normalizes that to `exitCode: 2` — so the validator every phase requires could never pass. Fixed at the script, not by loosening the contract, exactly as AC1 preferred: treating empty stdout as "clean" would have weakened the fail-closed guarantee for *every* validator. Defect 2: `parseValidatorOutput` ran `JSON.parse` on the whole trimmed stdout first, and a single NDJSON finding line **is** valid JSON — so it parsed, fell past the array/envelope branches, and hit `return null`, reporting one real gap as malformed. Two-plus lines only ever worked by accident (concatenated objects aren't valid JSON, throwing into the NDJSON fallback). Line count no longer changes what counts as a finding.

**The block, reproduced before fixing it.** `doctor` on the cherry-picked tree: `[FAIL] pack-signatures: 1 file(s) failed hash verification`. After `sign-content.mjs`: `[OK] pack-signatures: manifest + all file hashes verified`. That is the whole ticket in two lines.

**Mutations — and one that had to be corrected.** (A) Silencing the envelope again (`validator_exit() { exit 0; }`) reds both `run.test.ts` proofs: clean run → exitCode 0, and single real finding → exitCode 1 not 2. (B) The first attempt at Defect 2's mutation replaced the `return parseNdjson(trimmed)` inside the **catch block** — the pre-existing multi-line path — and redded the *two-line* test while the dedicated single-line fixture stayed green. That is a mutation aimed at the wrong subject, and it would have "verified" the wrong code. Retargeted at the **fallthrough** return (the actual Defect-2 fix), it reds the two tests that matter: `parses a single NDJSON finding line` and `treats a single non-envelope JSON object as one NDJSON finding`. Worth recording: breaking the subject only proves something when you break the *right* subject.

**AC4 holds** — `exitCode: 2` stays "verdict untrustworthy" and was not widened: empty stdout, non-JSON garbage, partially-parsing NDJSON, timeout and spawn error each still yield 2, each pinned by a test, all green.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **400 files / 2891 passed | 3 skipped** (+3), **59 e2e passed**. `doctor: OK`.

**Board: 118/118. Every ticket W0-01…W9-16 is `done`.**

2026-08-02 W10-01 done — the provider registry exists, and it is the same key the CLI already read. `packages/gateway/src/registry/**`, `apps/server/src/api/server/providers-{store,routes}.ts`. `cli/ops/providers-core.ts` said it verbatim — "There is no persisted provider registry yet anywhere in the codebase" — and read a bare `providers` settings key consumed only by `doctor` and `providers refresh`: no REST, no GUI, so registering a provider meant hand-editing JSON.

**The design decision worth recording: it stays that same settings key.** A new table would have needed a migration (`packages/events/migrations/**`, outside scope) and — far worse — created a second source of truth where the GUI and `doctor` disagree about which providers exist. Reusing the key inherits three guarantees rather than reimplementing them: run>project>global precedence (FR-S1), a `settings.changed` event per write (FR-S3), and the file layer's secret refusal (FR-S2). The round-trip test asserts the bytes land under `providers` in `.dokima/settings.json` — i.e. exactly where `getEffectiveSettings` looks.

**Credential refusal is at the boundary, not the store.** A literal key is rejected *wholesale before validation or persistence* — "stored then scrubbed" would both write it to disk and leave the caller believing it was accepted. Proved against the bytes on disk, not the API response.

**Two mutations, both red.** (1) Disabling the secret scan reds the literal-credential test alone. (2) Disabling the D-019 consent check reds both the unit and route copilot tests — Copilot cannot be enabled through the registry without a ledgered ack, and a *disabled* copilot entry is still allowed, so the gate is a gate rather than a wall.

**Prerequisite discharged (the one the plan flagged):** `packages/gateway/package.json` published only `./src/index.ts`, and that export map is the *stated* reason `harbormaster/src/loop-land-policy.ts` reimplements escalation policy locally instead of importing it. Now publishes `./registry`, `./routing` and `./escalation` as well, so the seam does not get reimplemented a fourth time.

**SCOPE NOTE.** Registering the routes required one import + one call in `settings-routes.ts`, outside the declared write_scope — the build-then-wire seam again (routes nobody can reach are a disconnected pipeline, not a deliverable). Same class as W9-15's note and W10-07's `TEST_SIBLING_STRICT`. Recorded, not silently widened.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **402 files / 2905 passed | 3 skipped** (+14), **59 e2e passed**, 7/7 boundary red fixtures proven failing-capable.

2026-08-02 W10-03 done — the model the user picks is now the model that runs. `apps/server/src/api/pipeline/model-resolution.ts` (new), `gateway-model-port.ts`. The defect, stated exactly as it was: the ONLY production model-call path in the product built a hardcoded `createOaiCompatProvider` from three environment variables, and neither call site imported the router, `resolveModelChain`, the matrix or the registry. So five finished, contract-tested adapters (W2-01…09) were never constructed on any production path — only by their own tests — and the matrix was read by `roster-resolve.ts` for DISPLAY only, meaning the UI could advertise a model the pipeline would never call.

**Resolution order is explicit and both directions are pinned:** an explicit registry+matrix selection wins; the env vars survive as the documented CI/fixture override (the e2e fake-model gateway depends on them) and deliberately LOSE to a real selection. With nothing configured, env still wins — a first run must keep working offline (C-1), not hard-fail.

**Model→provider binding uses the `<providerId>/<model>` prefix** already half-present in the codebase (`matrix-routes.ts` flags Copilot rows by a `copilot/` prefix). An unprefixed model binds when exactly one provider is enabled and is otherwise reported as ambiguous — never guessed. A disabled provider is refused rather than silently used.

**`route()` deliberately, not a bare `resolveModelChain`:** that keeps maker≠verifier STRUCTURAL (C-4/Law 5), so wiring the registry in cannot become a way around the guard. A verifier landing on the maker's model still throws `SameModelRefusedError` through the new path — asserted, not assumed.

**THE MUTATION IS THE ACCEPTANCE.** Reverting only the resolution call site to env-only — the exact pre-ticket behaviour — reds **8 of 11** seam tests, including "routes to the provider the MATRIX names, not the env default". That is the W9-11 test applied to the bug it would have caught, and it was executed, not asserted.

**One thing refused rather than faked.** `providerForConfig` now dispatches on kind, which makes ollama/lm-studio reachable from production for the first time. The cloud kinds (anthropic/openai/vertex/copilot) throw a NAMED refusal instead: `AnthropicConfig.costTable` is required with no $0 default ("no $0 default for a paid API" — its own header), and the adapter needs a resolved secret rather than the `credentialRef` the registry stores. Fabricating either would have been a lie about cost or a credential leak. A user selecting a cloud provider gets an explanation, never a silent fallback to localhost. **HANDOFF:** keychain resolution + a real price table is the follow-up that makes cloud kinds constructible.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **403 files / 2918 passed | 3 skipped** (+13), **59 e2e passed**.

2026-08-02 W10-06 done — a real token scale, no raw hexes, and a board that fits its pane. `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `styles.test.ts`, 14 feature stylesheets, `board/board.css`. Five tokens became 38; 54 hex literals outside `styles.css` became zero.

**The defect was worse than "hardcoded colours", and the survey is what found it.** Eight tokens — `--sw-success`, `--sw-warning`, `--sw-attention`, `--sw-muted`, `--sw-fg-muted`, `--color-text-muted`, `--color-border`, `--color-warning` — were referenced through `var(--x, #literal)` and **defined nowhere**. Every one of those fallbacks always won, so roughly a third of the "tokenised" declarations were rendering a frozen literal while reading as theme-aware. The same role was improvised per file: six different reds, five ambers, five greens.

**Six live WCAG 2.2 AA failures fell out of that, and the migration fixes them.** Muted text had no dark value at all — `#666` on the dark background is **3.20:1**. The active Settings tab put `#fff` on `var(--sw-accent)`, which flips to a *light* blue in dark theme: **2.58:1**. Three improvised warning/attention colours were below 4.5:1 in light theme (`#e0a800` at **2.15:1**, `#b8860b` at 3.25:1, `#d64545` at 4.38:1), and the notification badge's white-on-`#d64545` was 4.38:1. Every replacement value was measured against the background it actually sits on, in both themes, before being committed — the ratios are in `styles.css` beside each token. Axe never caught these because the affected states (stale/waived badges) aren't in the seeded fixtures.

**The gate is the deliverable, and it was written before the migration** so it was red against all 54 violations on its first run rather than green-by-construction afterwards. Hex is legal only on a `--sw-*` definition line in `styles.css` — no allowlist, nothing to argue about. Raw px gets three exempt properties (`box-shadow`, `background-size`, `background-position`), each with a written reason, because a px there is shadow/gradient geometry and not a spacing step. A fourth assertion bans `var()` fallbacks outright, since that is precisely where the eight phantom tokens hid. **Three planted defects verified red:** a raw hex, a raw px, and a re-introduced phantom fallback. A fifth test guards the guard — a bad glob would make the whole thing vacuously pass, which is how a lint rule dies quietly.

**The board fits now, and the fix is grid rather than `flex-wrap` for a measured reason.** Six 12rem columns needed ~1200px in a pane that is **471px** at 1440x900; the shipped tour capture shows "In Pro…" clipped with columns 4-6 off-pane entirely, and *both* board e2e specs force a 2400px viewport to work around it. `flex-wrap` was tried first and rejected on the screenshot: `flex-grow` stretches whatever lands on the last row, so a 4+2 wrap rendered Blocked and Done at double width and read as a rendering bug. `auto-fit` + `minmax(--sw-board-column-min, 1fr)` gives equal columns and aligned rows. 9rem yields 2 per row at 219px; 8.5rem fits 3 at 143px but wrapped "Move ticket E2E-1" mid-identifier — the same defect class the ticket exists to remove — so 9rem it is.

**The W9-05 edge fade was checked for deadness, not assumed alive.** Leaving its declarations in place purely to green `Lane.test.tsx` (which reads raw CSS text and is outside this ticket's write_scope) would have been exactly the kind of fossil Law 4 exists to prevent. Measured instead: `.split-pane__pane` is draggable well below one column's minimum, and at a 120px pane the strip scrolls 128px of content against a 95px viewport. The affordance still has a width range where it does its job.

**PROCESS TRAP, recorded because it cost a gate cycle.** Claiming the ticket with `JSON.stringify(plan, null, 2)` rewrote **460 lines** of `plan.json`: the file is stored ASCII-escaped (`§`, `—`) and naive re-serialisation emits literal UTF-8. W9-11's byte-preservation test caught it — "expected 0 to be greater than 2000" — but only in the full suite. Use `writePlan()` from `scripts/conductor-lib.mjs`, never a bare stringify. Trap #4 also landed as advertised: `pnpm test` was green while `tsc` had three errors in the new test file.

**APPEARANCE CHANGED, not just contrast — say so before someone diffs screenshots.** Consolidating six reds, five ambers and five greens onto three roles visibly restyles things that were never contrast failures: the Fleet heartbeat dot goes from `#2ecc71` neon to `#1a7f37` forest in light theme, its stale variant from `#e67e22` orange to `#b45309` amber, and the roster fitness-badge borders from `#3a3`/`#a33`/`#aa3` to the semantic set. **Both themes were rendered and looked at, not just measured** — Fleet, Board, Settings and Roster in dark, plus the Fleet card in light. The two fixes are visible: dark-theme stat labels went from barely-there grey to legible, and the active Settings tab is now dark-on-blue instead of white-on-light-blue.

**The tour captures under `docs/tour/img/` are now stale in two ways** and should not be read as current: they predate the Dokima rename (the header still says "Shipwright") and `05-board-seeded.png` shows a six-across board layout the code no longer produces. `board.css` cites that image as the evidence for the clipping defect, which is correct as history. `docs/tour/` is outside this ticket's write_scope, so regenerating it needs its own ticket.

**HONEST SCOPE NOTE.** This is the token/layout pass only. 112 spacing declarations still sit off the 0.25rem grid and four font sizes sit off the type scale — snapping those changes layout, which is a design decision rather than a script, so only exact on-scale values were migrated (zero visual drift, asserted by the migration script itself). That work plus the typography-hierarchy defects the tour exposes is **filed as W10-28**, as this ticket's acceptance required rather than smuggled in here.

Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning), typecheck clean, **403 files / 2923 passed | 3 skipped** (+5), **59 e2e passed**, 3/3 planted design-gate defects proven failing-capable.

2026-08-02 W10-05 done — UX_SPEC gains the provider/model surface it never had. `docs/design/UX_SPEC.md` 209 → 318 lines; new §6a plus two rows in §2b's empty-state table. §6 had promised that "provider onboarding … lives here (D-007)" and then never said what *here* looked like, so W10-04 would have been invented at the keyboard.

**The spec is written against shapes that already exist, not against an imagined API.** Every field, kind and refusal binds to `packages/gateway/src/registry/` (`ProviderKind`, `ProviderEntry`, the `rule` string on `ProviderRegistryError`) and the three live routes. The field set is specified as a *function of the kind*, which is what the code already enforces: only `oai-compat` requires a `baseUrl` (`ENDPOINT_KINDS`), ollama/lm-studio prefill an editable localhost default, and the fixed-endpoint cloud kinds show no endpoint field at all.

**Quoted copy is verbatim, and that is checked rather than intended.** The cloud-kind refusal and the maker≠verifier refusal appear in the spec as the exact strings `gateway-model-port.ts` and `routing/maker-verifier.ts` raise, and the cited rule names (`missing-base-url`, `invalid-base-url-scheme`, `consent-required`, …) all exist in `registry/validate.ts`. A refusal the user reads should be the refusal the code raised; a spec that paraphrases its own error messages licenses implementation to soften them.

**Eight states are written out, and the distinctions between them are the point.** Unreachable, reachable-but-serves-nothing, and offline-so-the-bundled-catalog-answers are three different things that all look like "no models" if you let them collapse — the honest-absence rule (W9-15) applied to discovery. A selected model that vanishes from an endpoint is marked missing rather than silently rewritten or dropped, and a disabled provider stays visible and greyed because a refusal to *use* is not a reason to *hide*.

**Two destructive affordances have their copy pinned so implementation cannot soften them.** The Copilot consent (D-019) names the undocumented `copilot_internal` API and says plainly that enforcement can permanently ban the user's GitHub account, that the risk is theirs and not Dokima's, and that enabling is ledgered; declining leaves the provider registered and disabled, never half-enabled. Removing a provider states what it does NOT touch — the keychain entry survives, the endpoint's own models survive, and prior receipts survive because the log is append-only (C-6).

2026-08-02 W10-02 done — model catalog + discovery: `listModels()` already existed and `dokima providers refresh` already called it, then discarded the result after computing `modelCount`. New `packages/gateway/src/catalog/` module (`resolveProviderCatalog`) persists and exposes it, backed by a bundled offline catalog (`content/model-catalog/catalog.v1.json`, versioned + provenance-headed like the rest of `content/`) for zero-network first run (Law 9 / C-1).

**"Persist" was implemented as carry-through-and-expose, not durable storage.** The write_scope (widened onto its own acceptance-named files by the prior claim commit) has no store or API route in it — `GET /models` from `docs/API_DESIGN.md` and the registry-backed persistence are later tickets' job (W10-04's Providers panel is the documented consumer). This ticket's surface is the reusable resolver both of those build on: `resolveProviderCatalog()` returns which source answered (`'discovered'` vs `'bundled'`) and, when unreachable, the failure reason — never a silently empty list, never a fabricated one (W9-15's honest-absence rule applied to discovery, per `docs/design/UX_SPEC.md` §6a). `dokima providers refresh` now prints the actual model ids instead of just a count, and distinguishes reachable-but-empty from unreachable from offline-bundled-fallback — three states that all look like "no models" if collapsed.

**`oai-compat` deliberately has no bundled entry.** Ollama (a real public library) and LM Studio (matched to the ids already recorded in `packages/gateway/src/providers/fixtures.ts`) get bundled reference lists; an arbitrary user-supplied OpenAI-compatible endpoint has no knowable catalog, so unreachable + `source: null` + reason is the honest answer, not an oversight.

**Bundled catalog provenance is stated, not implied.** The JSON file's own `provenance` field says the entries are curated by the implementing agent from training-data knowledge of each ecosystem as of 2026-08-02, not fetched from any live registry — `models.dev`-style remote discovery was evaluated and explicitly not built (acceptance criterion: "a remote registry may be optional; it may never be required"). Contract-tested against the recorded LM Studio/Ollama `/v1/models` fixture via the real `OaiCompatProvider` factories (`createLmStudioProvider`/`createOllamaProvider`), never a live call.

2026-08-02 W10-02 retry — external verification of the first "done" pass found `resolveProviderCatalog`'s catch block called `loadBundled(kind)` unguarded: a bundled-catalog load failure (malformed/missing `content/model-catalog/catalog.v1.json`, or a raw ENOENT) would reject the promise instead of resolving, contradicting `resolve.ts`'s own "never silently empty" contract and crashing every provider in `dokima providers refresh`'s `Promise.all` rather than reporting just the affected one unreachable. Fixed by wrapping the `loadBundled` call in its own try/catch inside `resolveProviderCatalog`'s existing catch block: a bundled-load failure now degrades to `{status: 'unreachable', source: null, models: [], reason: <discovery error> + <bundled-load error>}` instead of rejecting. New RED FIXTURE test injects a throwing loader and asserts the promise still resolves with that shape. `resolveAsset()`'s own possible failure to locate the distribution root happens at module-import time (`MODEL_CATALOG_CONTENT_PATH` is a top-level const, same pattern as `fitness/fixtures.ts`'s `FIXTURES_DIR`) and is a pre-existing cross-cutting risk outside this ticket's `write_scope`, not something `resolveProviderCatalog` can catch after the fact.

''}${urlPath}`, but every `urlPath` in that module already hardcodes `/api/v1` — App.tsx passes `baseUrl="/api/v1"` into `EvidencePanel`/`CommandPalette` (correct for `board/api.ts`, whose urlPaths carry no prefix and rely on baseUrl for it), so those two consumers doubled it while `ArtifactViewerRoot.tsx` (no baseUrl passed, outside this ticket's `write_scope`) stayed correct by accident. 2026-08-02 W10-30 done — anchors had no colour rule anywhere in `apps/web/src/**/*.css`, so every `<a>` fell back to the UA default `#0000EE`: 1.95:1 against `--sw-bg` in the dark theme (needs 4.5:1), 8.59:1 in light. Author styles beat the UA stylesheet regardless of specificity, so this also overrides the default `:visited` purple with no extra rule.

2026-08-02 W10-32 done — `.sr-only` was applied at five call sites (`board/Card.tsx`, `drawer/DagEditPanel.tsx`, `SpendByRung.tsx`, `ContractPanel.tsx`, `palette/CommandPalette.tsx`) and defined in zero of the app's fifteen stylesheets, so every board card printed "Move ticket E2E-1" as visible product copy above a `<select>` already labelled "Move to…" — screen-reader users heard the label twice, sighted users saw a bug on the signature surface, in both themes, on all six seeded board captures. Fixed once, globally: `.sr-only` in `styles.css` using the standard visually-hidden technique (`clip-path: inset(50%)` plus a legacy `clip: rect(0 0 0 0)`, `position: absolute` with a non-zero box so it stays in the accessibility tree — never `display: none`, which would also break the existing `getByLabel('Move ticket E2E-DRAG-1')` a11y e2e assertion that already depended on this text being programmatically associated). Sized off `--sw-space-0-5` rather than a bare `1px` so the rule doesn't reintroduce the raw-px pattern the W10-06 gate bans outside the token block.

**The causal link to the board column floor, confirmed by re-measurement, not assumed.** `board.css` recorded that 8.5rem "wrapped 'Move ticket E2E-1' mid-identifier," which is why W10-06 raised `--sw-board-column-min` to 9rem, dropping the six lifecycle states from 3 columns per row to 2. That measurement was taken against text that was never meant to paint. With `.sr-only` now defined, re-verified directly against the real server + real event log (six tickets seeded, one per lifecycle column) at 1440x900 and 1280x800, both themes, `--sw-board-column-min` back at 8.5rem: all six columns render unclipped, no wrapped identifiers, zero visible "Move ticket" text in the DOM. 1440 goes 3-per-row (each track 143px, matching the comment's own "8.5rem fits 3 columns per row at 143px each"); 1280 stays 2-per-row — arithmetic, not a judgment call: the board pane is ~393px wide at 1280, and three 8.5rem tracks need 3×136 + 2×8(gap) = 424px, which doesn't fit at either 8.5rem or the old 9rem (3×144+16 = 448px) — 1280 was 2-per-row before this ticket and is 2-per-row after; only 1440 (2→3) changes. Floor dropped back to 8.5rem; the pipeline reads as six states again at the width where it always could.

**RED FIXTURE, per acceptance**: same blind spot as W10-30/W10-31, one level up — those guard a missing *property* on a known selector, this guards a missing *selector* entirely. `styles.test.ts` scans every `.tsx` for a literal `className="sr-only"` (proving the check isn't vacuous) and every stylesheet for a `.sr-only` rule; reverting just the new CSS rule reds the "is defined in at least one stylesheet" assertion — verified directly: deleting the `.sr-only` rule from `styles.css` while leaving its own explanatory comment (and board.css's, both of which write the literal string `.sr-only` in prose) intact reds exactly that one assertion, 29 passed. Scoped to an explicit `UTILITY_CLASSES` list (currently just `sr-only`), not a fully generic className sweep — the codebase has ~60 other classes applied with no matching CSS rule that are legitimate unstyled semantic hooks (e.g. `board-card__type`), and flagging those would be false positives outside this ticket's `write_scope`. Five gate-integrity tests (docs/TESTING.md §6) feed synthetic component/stylesheet pairs through the same helpers: catches the real-world shape, doesn't false-positive on a longer class sharing the prefix (`.sr-only-legacy`) or on the class name appearing only in a comment, passes once defined, and doesn't flag a class no component applies.

**Gate-integrity fix (first attempt's escaped bug, caught by external review before this pass)**: the first pass's `classIsDefined()` regex-matched `.sr-only` against the raw stylesheet text, comments included — and this ticket's own explanatory comments (`styles.css`, `board.css`) write the literal string `.sr-only` in prose right next to the real rule. Verified empirically before fixing: deleting the real rule while leaving those comments in place left all 29 tests green, because the prose satisfied the same regex the gate used to detect the rule — the exact false-pass a reviewer would need to demonstrate to fail this ticket, and it worked. Fixed by stripping `/* ... */` comments before matching and requiring the match be followed by an actual rule opener (`[^{}]*\{`), plus a new red-fixture test that reproduces the exact comment-adjacent-to-rule shape and asserts it still reads as undefined once the rule itself is gone. Re-ran the same delete-the-rule experiment after the fix: the suite now reds on the intended assertion instead of passing (see RED FIXTURE paragraph above).

Board layout re-verified directly (real server, real event log, all six lifecycle states seeded): 3-per-row at 1440x900, 2-per-row (unclipped) at 1280x800, both themes, zero visible "Move ticket" text in the DOM.

Wrapped the panel in a new `.shortcuts-overlay__backdrop` — fixed, full-viewport `rgb(0 0 0 / 45%)` scrim, matching `command-palette__backdrop`'s existing pattern exactly — that flex-centers the panel with `padding-top: 9rem`. Fixed rem, not `vh`: the header stack is a content-driven height (app-shell header + Fleet's own toolbar), not a viewport-proportional one, so a `vh` offset that clears it at 1440x900 can clip it again at 1280x800. No focus trap added — kept `role="dialog"` with no `aria-modal` and no forced focus move, since neither is paired with the other here (unlike `TicketDrawer`/`CommandPalette`, which both move focus into the panel on open): `aria-modal` alone would hide the rest of the page from assistive tech while focus stayed parked on a now-hidden element, which is worse than today, not better. Escape-to-close (`isShortcutOverlayCloseKey`, pre-existing) is untouched and the `app.spec.ts` e2e assertion for it still passes unmodified.

`shortcuts.ts:14-17`'s static two-row list (`?`, `Esc`) never mentioned ⌘K/Ctrl+K even though `CommandPalette` (`palette/shortcuts.ts`, W4-12) is real and wired inside every open project, and said nothing when Cmd+K is inert on the Fleet screen either. The static `SHORTCUTS` export is now `shortcutsFor({ paletteActive })`, a pure function that appends the palette row only when the palette is actually mounted. `ShortcutsOverlay.tsx` computes `paletteActive` from the URL directly — `?project=` present AND no `?view=` layered over it, mirroring `App.tsx`'s own `CommandPalette` mount condition (verified against `App.tsx:290-320`'s view ternary: Roster/Settings/Plan/Trace/Wizard all replace the project canvas and unmount the palette with it) — rather than a prop threaded down, since the overlay mounts outside `App.tsx`'s project/view ternary by design and `App.tsx` sits outside this ticket's `write_scope`.

`styles.css:200-204`'s `<dt>` had no width, so each row's key/description boundary depended on that row's own `<kbd>` text — "?" and "Esc" render at different x positions (measured x=577/x=593), worsening the moment a third row lands. Replaced the per-row `.shortcuts-overlay__row` flex wrapper with a shared `.shortcuts-overlay__list` grid (`grid-template-columns: auto 1fr`, the same pattern as `.ticket-drawer__contract-grid`) — `<dt>`/`<dd>` are now direct grid children via a keyed `Fragment` per row (the pattern already used in `layout/SplitPaneWorkspace.tsx`, not a `display: contents` hack), so column alignment is structural, not row-local.

`trace.css` had no `.trace-event__type` rule, so the raw dotted event id inherited full `--sw-fg` at base size while its siblings (`__pass`, `__actor`, `__time`) were already muted — the least human-meaningful token was the loudest thing in the row. `.trace-event__type` now joins the same muted selector list. Timestamps rendered raw ISO-8601 UTC with milliseconds, unlocalised, with no step delta; `TraceView.tsx` now renders via `toLocaleString()` plus a `+Xms`/`+X.Ys`/`+XmYYs` delta since the previous row's timestamp — the fixture's ~1ms spacing is real replay data, left alone rather than "fixed."

2026-08-03 W10-27 done — the durable half of the 2026-08-02 release blocker: history scanning joined the gate. `content/validators/secrets-scan.sh` (W3-13, SC-06) greps the working **tree** and passes `--exclude-dir=.git`, so by construction it can never see a credential that was committed and later deleted — which is precisely how the Ed25519 content-signing private key sat in pushed history from 2026-07-20 to 2026-08-02 with every gate green the entire time. New `scripts/validate-history-secrets.mjs` reads every blob reachable from `git rev-list --objects --all`, using the same six categories as the tree scanner and the same masking rule (a matched value is never printed — `ghp_...REDACTED(n chars)` — because a scanner must not become the leak it exists to prevent, Law 8). **No external scanning binary**: git plumbing plus Node, against the W10_PLAN spec's assumed gitleaks dependency — measured at ~1.1s over 1064 commits / 3952 reachable blobs / 295 MB, so the dependency bought nothing and would have cost a prerequisite that is absent on most machines (Law 9); binary blobs are skipped by git's own NUL-in-first-8000-bytes heuristic, the `I` in the tree scanner's `grep -rEIn`. Exit `0` clean / `1` findings / `2` could-not-scan, the validator-pack contract. **BASELINE KEYS ON THE VALUE, NOT THE PATH** — `scripts/history-secrets-baseline.json` matches on `category + sha256(value)[0:16]` alone, because a path-scoped allowlist would let a real credential hide inside an already-baselined fixture file and would churn on every edit to one (blob shas change; values don't). 24 entries, each reviewed by hand rather than bulk-accepted: 21 test fixtures, and 3 documentation examples that are AWS's own published `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE`, a `postgres://admin:hunter2@` doc sample, and a genuine false positive on the prose string `sk-at-most-one-question` in `ux-engineer.md`. **The scan also independently confirms the 2026-08-02 purge worked**: no PEM private key under `.keys/` appears anywhere in reachable history. **FAILS CLOSED**: `actions/checkout@v4` defaults to `fetch-depth: 1`, and with no history the scan would pass vacuously — a gate that cannot fail — so the CI job sets `fetch-depth: 0` **and** the script independently refuses (exit 2, never 0) on a shallow clone, an empty repo, a non-repo, or an unparseable baseline. RED FIXTURES (`scripts/validate-history-secrets.test.mjs`, 13 tests): a PEM key is committed, then deleted from the tree and gitignored — the exact 2026-08-02 shape — and `secrets-scan.sh`, **first proven to catch that same key while it is still in the tree** (the control that makes its later silence mean *blind* rather than *broken invocation*), then reads clean while this scanner exits 1 and hands over `git log --find-object=<blob>`, verified to resolve to the planting commit; a second unbaselined token added to an already-baselined fixture file still fails; neither stream ever contains a planted value. Fixture secrets in that file are assembled at runtime rather than written as literals — a literal would be committed once and then gate this repo forever, forcing a permanent baseline entry for a test's own fixture (a deliberate divergence from the older `secrets-scan-validator.test.ts`, whose literals are in today's baseline). Four planted defects against the scanner itself — shallow check disabled, first-match-only instead of `matchAll`, raw value instead of masked, unreadable baseline reading as clean — were each confirmed to turn the suite red before the fix was restored. Deliberately NOT added to `DEFAULT_REQUIRED_VALIDATORS`: the close gate's `classifySecretsGaps` calibration filters gaps to session-changed paths (D-014/L-28), which is right for a tree scan and actively wrong for history findings that are commit-scoped — that seam needs its own ticket, not a widened `write_scope` here. `docs/TESTING.md` §6a documents the tier and the four properties; `docs/RELEASE_TRACKER.md`'s pre-public checklist records that re-running this by hand before a tag is now a confirmation, not the control. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning in `docs/dogfood/run-dogfood.mjs`, unrelated), typecheck clean across 14 workspace projects, **415 files / 3102 passed | 3 skipped** (+1 file / +13 tests net), **61 e2e passed**; `validate-plan.mjs` / `validate-traceability.mjs` both clean.
2026-08-03 (2nd) W10-27 follow-up — closed a hole in my own deliverable, found by writing the commit message for the first one. The scanner enumerated `git rev-list --objects --all` and then filtered to `type === 'blob'`, so **commit messages were never scanned**. `git rev-list --objects` prints commit objects with *no path*, so the parser's `if (sp > 0)` guard silently discarded every one of them — the same class of defect as the ticket it was fixing: a scanner that looks confident while structurally blind to a whole category of history. A credential pasted into a commit message is published exactly like one in a file, and no tree edit removes it. `reachableBlobs` became `reachableObjects`: commits are kept (path `null`), annotated tags are added from `git for-each-ref refs/tags` (rev-list does not enumerate them), and the type filter now admits `blob`/`commit`/`tag`; findings report `commit message <sha7>` with a `git cat-file -p` hint instead of the blob-only `--find-object`. Object count over this repo went 3960 → 5025 (+1065 commit messages), still 0 gating, runtime ~1.3s. Two new red fixtures — a token in a commit message and one in an annotated tag message, each with a spotless working tree — and reverting the filter to `type === 'blob'` was confirmed to red exactly those two. Docs corrected in `TESTING.md` §1/§6a and `RELEASE_TRACKER.md`, which had all inherited the "every blob" phrasing. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **415 files / 3104 passed | 3 skipped** (+2 tests), **61 e2e passed**.
2026-08-03 (3rd) W10-27 follow-up — the CI job was trusting `fetch-depth: 0` to hand it a complete history, and that trust was unverified. Simulated both refspecs against a bare origin carrying a leaked `sk-` token on a second branch: with `+refs/heads/main:refs/remotes/origin/main` the scanner printed **"OK: no un-baselined credential shapes in git history"** over a repo that demonstrably has one. It is not shallow, so the shallowness check never fires — the denominator is just silently smaller, which is the precise failure mode this gate exists to kill, reproduced inside the gate itself. Fixed at both ends rather than one: the CI job now runs an explicit `git fetch --prune --no-tags origin '+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*'` before scanning (so coverage does not depend on what `actions/checkout` happens to fetch), **and** the scanner refuses to trust that it did — `--verify-remote-refs` compares `git ls-remote --heads` against the local ref set and exits 2 naming any branch it cannot see. That flag is opt-in precisely because it is the one network call in the file: CI has already talked to the remote it cloned from, while a local run stays offline (Law 9). Output now reports the ref count as well as the object count, so a narrowed denominator is visible in every log. RED FIXTURE (16 tests total): the narrow clone is built for real, the blind `OK` is asserted as the defect, `--verify-remote-refs` is asserted to exit 2 naming `feature`, and the token is then found once the missing branch is fetched — all three states in one test, so it cannot pass by accident. Two limits now stated in `TESTING.md` §6a and `RELEASE_TRACKER.md` rather than left implied: the six categories are shared with the tree scanner, so an unpatterned credential shape is invisible to both; and `--all` is reachable-only, so a force-pushed and garbage-collected secret reads clean locally while it may still be retrievable from the forge's dangling objects — the same caveat the 2026-08-02 write-up records about what a rewrite does not undo. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **415 files / 3105 passed | 3 skipped** (+1 test), **61 e2e passed**.
2026-08-03 W10-43 done — the published tarball could not find its own assets. Claiming `@bpmforge/dokima` on npm (founder-authorized; 0.1.0 chosen by the founder) required scoping the root package name, and `packages/shared/src/paths/index.ts:31` identified the distribution root by matching `package.json` `name === 'dokima'` exactly. Scoping the name made `distributionRoot()` throw `DistributionRootNotFoundError` at module-evaluation time, so `content/`, both migration sets and the built web dist all became unreachable: `npm install` succeeded and then `dokima` died on startup. Found by actually packing and installing the tarball into a clean project — `npm pack --dry-run` listed a perfectly correct 240-file, 928 kB tarball and told me nothing, because the file list was never the problem. **Fixed the marker, not the string**: a name allowlist would have fixed this one rename and re-armed the trap for the next, so the root is now identified by the manifest declaring the `dokima` **bin** — the thing that actually makes a manifest *the distribution*. Both the source monorepo root and the published package carry it; an `@dokima/*` workspace manifest does not (preserving the existing "walks PAST @dokima/* manifests" property), and neither does a consumer's own `package.json`, which is exactly what sits above a real install. `isRootManifest` is now exported, because the probe starts from `import.meta.url` and `DOKIMA_DIST_ROOT` short-circuits it — the rule was unreachable from a test, which is why a rename got as far as a real install before anything noticed. +6 tests; reverting to name-equality reds 5, including the two pre-existing `distributionRoot` tests whose own assertion had encoded the identical brittle coupling (`expect(manifest.name).toBe('dokima')`) and is now asserted on the bin marker. Publish metadata added to the root `package.json`: scoped name, `0.1.0`, `publishConfig.access: public` (scoped packages default to restricted and would otherwise fail or bill), description/license/repository/homepage/bugs/keywords, and `prepublishOnly` running the build so a tarball can never ship without `apps/server/dist/main.js` or `apps/web/dist` (both are `files` entries that npm omits silently when missing). `.gitignore` now excludes `*.tgz` so a pack artifact cannot be committed. **VERIFIED ON A REAL INSTALL, twice** — pack, install into a clean project outside this repo, run the binary: it boots (`dokima: listening at http://127.0.0.1:4733`), serves the built web dist, materializes `packs/` under a fresh `DOKIMA_HOME`, and answers `GET /api/v1/projects` → `200 {"projects":[]}` with a real bearer token. Confirmed still open and filed as W10-44: `dokima --help` boots the server rather than printing usage — re-confirmed against the packaged install, and it matters more once `npx` makes it a stranger's first command. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean across 14 workspace projects, **415 files / 3111 passed | 3 skipped** (+6 net), **61 e2e passed**; history-secrets scan clean.
2026-08-03 W10-44 done — `dokima --help` booted the server instead of printing usage. `runPackagedCli`'s final statement was an unconditional `return runServerBoot(io, deps)`, so every unrecognised argument — `--help`, `--version`, a typo, or a known command missing its subcommand — fell through to detecting/binding a port and opening a browser. Measured against the packaged install, not inferred from source: `dokima --help` printed `dokima is already running at http://127.0.0.1:4317 — opening the Canvas` and never exited. Harmless in a source checkout; it is the first command a stranger runs after `npx @bpmforge/dokima`, which is what made it worth a ticket now rather than staying a Known-gaps line. Argument handling now runs BEFORE dispatch and never falls through: `--help`/`-h` print a real usage block (commands + the four `DOKIMA_*` env vars) and exit 0; `--version`/`-V` print the version read from the distribution's own manifest via `resolveAsset` (so it works from both a source checkout and an installed package — W10-43's marker fix is what makes that resolvable) and exit 0; an unknown first token prints `unknown command '<x>'` plus usage to **stderr** and exits **2**, never 0, because a script must be able to tell a typo from a real run; and `packs`/`providers` without their required subcommand now refuse with their own usage line instead of silently booting. Bare `dokima` is untouched — this ticket adds an argument surface, it does not change the default. 2026-08-03 W10-49 done — `validate-file-size` now gates repo-wide, the follow-up W10-40 sequenced after the count reached zero. **Nothing new is scanned**: `runValidators` (`scripts/conductor/session.mjs`) always ran each validator over the whole worktree and then discarded every finding whose detail did not mention a file in the ticket's `git diff`. Going repo-wide deleted that post-filter for one named validator — `it.detail && (repoWide || changed.some(...))` — so the change costs nothing to run; it stops throwing away what was already measured. **Opt-in per validator, not global**, via `validators.repoWide: ["validate-file-size"]`: `validate-circular-deps` also gates and the advisory set is explicitly noisy grep-heuristics (the config's own `$note`), so dropping the filter for everything would have buried real findings under pre-existing debt. The reasoning is recorded in that `$note` rather than left implicit, because **repo-wide is only honest at a zero count** — then any finding is necessarily something the current ticket introduced. If the count ever goes non-zero again, every ticket fails for debt it did not create (the self-DoS W10-40 warned about when the two largest over-cap files were the gate-runner itself), and the note says to remove the entry rather than waive it. RED FIXTURE drives the **real** `runValidators` against a real temp worktree running the real `validate-file-size.sh`, not a mirrored copy of the selection predicate — a mirror would have passed even with the production check deleted, which is precisely this bug's shape. Four cases: a 450-line file the ticket never touched now reaches the gate; the same out-of-diff finding is still discarded for a validator left diff-scoped (so a bug disabling filtering everywhere fails this half); this repo is asserted to be at the zero count the opt-in depends on, so the day that stops holding it fails here rather than on someone else's ticket; and the config opts in file-size and only file-size. 2026-08-03 W10-53 done — filed from an external conductor-pilot report the founder supplied (`~/Documents/CONDUCTOR_PILOT_REPORT_REDACTED_2026-08-03.md`). **Both harness bugs that report documents were checked against Dokima and NEITHER applies** — checked against the code, not assumed. (1) Its glob-normalization bug (a `dir/*.test.ts` scope silently treated as a literal nonexistent directory, costing two tickets): `globToRegex` handles that shape correctly. (2) Its commit-ordering bug — the harness committed only *after* all gates passed, so every review round ran against an uncommitted tree while the review prompt claimed to check committed work, structurally guaranteeing rejection of correct work on any ticket needing a fix cycle: **structurally impossible here**, because Dokima's coding prompt instructs the agent to commit ("commit in small steps") and `conductor/gates.mjs:28` already hard-fails on `no commits on ticket branch`, so an empty `main...branch` diff cannot pass vacuously. **The residue was real, though, and bigger than expected.** Two independent glob implementations decide `write_scope` — `globToRegex` ENFORCES it at gate time, `validate-plan.mjs`'s `globOverlaps` decides lane-overlap legality at board time — kept in sync by a comment ("keep in sync; W3-12 consolidates"). A differential test across a hand-picked matrix plus **every write_scope glob actually on the board** crossed against real repo paths found a genuine divergence on its first run: `apps/web/src/**/*.css` vs `apps/web/src/styles.css` — board validator **true**, enforcer **false**. `globToRegex` expanded `a/**/b` to `^a/.*/b$`, leaving the slash literal, so `**` required **at least one** intervening directory, while bash globstar, minimatch, git pathspec and validate-plan.mjs all treat it as zero-or-more. **It had already bitten three times without being noticed**: W10-06, W10-28 and W10-30 all carried `apps/web/src/**/*.css` and every one of them silently worked around it by listing `styles.css` explicitly. That is precisely the pilot report's failure shape — a scope the board accepts and the enforcer rejects, diagnosed as a bad ticket rather than a dialect gap — and an enforcement boundary narrower than it reads is the dangerous direction, because it trains operators to widen scopes by hand. Fixed by making `**/` span zero-or-more segments via `(?:[^/]*\/)*`, with the `?` expansion **reordered before** the sentinel joins: the replacement contains a literal `?` and the later blanket `?` → `[^/]` pass ate it, producing `([^/]:…)` — silently wrong rather than a syntax error, caught only because the verification ran the matcher instead of reading it. Regression case pins both directions: `**/` now spans zero segments without over-widening (still extension-bound, still start-anchored, `?` still exactly one character). `write_scope` amended mid-ticket and recorded — the **third** amendment this session, all the same habit §10 of RESUME_2026-08-03 names. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **417 files / 3145 passed | 3 skipped** (+3 net), **61 e2e passed**, `validate-file-size` clean, `conductor --lint` exits 0.
2026-08-03 W10-52 done — **a release blocker, found while auditing for release-readiness, and worse than the ticket was filed as.** Filed from W10-51 reconciliation as "the shared validator libs are unsigned". Probing rather than trusting that framing showed they are not merely unsigned: they are **never installed**. `packsUpdate` installs exactly the files `content/manifest.json` names, and `sign-content.mjs` selected them with the pack-DISCOVERY pattern `^(?:(?:validate|run)-.+|secrets-scan)\.sh$`, which `_lib.sh` and `_lib_sdlc_config.sh` do not match. A real install therefore landed **81 validators and zero shared libraries**, and every one of those 81 sources `_lib.sh`. **Proven at runtime, not inferred**: executing an installed validator from a real `packsUpdate` destination exits **127** — `_lib.sh: No such file or directory`, then `validator_init: command not found`, then `detect_project_root: command not found`. The shipped validator pack was non-functional in its entirety. **Why nothing caught it, which is the real deliverable:** the repo runs validators from `content/validators/` in the SOURCE TREE, where the libraries sit beside them, so every gate, every conductor run and all 3147 tests passed throughout. `packs-update.test.ts` verified signatures, hashes and install counts — and never once EXECUTED an installed validator. A pack could be fully verified, fully installed, and completely broken at the same time; counting files is not evidence they run. Same shape as the W10-29 finding (a green suite over a surface that 404'd for every user). Fixed by widening the signer to `_lib.*\.sh`, which both ships them and brings them under the signature — independently correct, since an attacker who could rewrite `_lib.sh` controlled all 81 validators while every signature still verified. Two new gates: one **executes** an installed validator and asserts it exits on its own 0/1/2 contract rather than 127, with `command not found` and `No such file or directory` asserted absent; the other asserts every installed validator's sourced library is present AND in `verifiedFiles`, not merely copied. Both proven red against the pre-fix manifest. Manifest 81 → 83 entries, re-signed, signature verified. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **417 files / 3147 passed | 3 skipped** (+2 net), **61 e2e passed**, `validate-file-size` clean.

2026-08-03 W10-57 — local provider timeout raised 60s → 300s; necessary, and proven insufficient. `DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` in `oai-compat-types.ts` is the LOCAL path (`createLmStudioProvider` and `createOllamaProvider` are both OaiCompat variants), and at 60s the product's headline use case did not work: driving the real installed product against LM Studio, the creation pipeline died with `pipeline-run: request timed out after 60000ms`. A default tuned for a fast hosted API that times out on the very model the setup wizard just configured contradicts C-1 and the README's claim that a local box is "a first-class setup, not a downgrade". **The cloud adapters deliberately keep 60s** — anthropic, copilot and openai talk to hosted endpoints where a minute of silence really does mean something is wrong — and a test asserts that, so a local raise cannot quietly become a global one. Fixtures assert BOTH directions, because raising a timeout until nothing trips it is removing the guard, not fixing it: a response slower than the old 60s default now succeeds (proven red against the old constant), and one past the new default still raises `ProviderTimeoutError`. **Then the honest half**: with 300s the same run failed at `300000ms`. Measured the floor directly — one pipeline-sized call to qwen3.5-9b on this hardware takes **33 seconds and 3,088 completion tokens, 2,987 of them reasoning** — and the pipeline issues several sequentially over growing prompts. There is no constant that is both large enough for a local reasoning model and small enough to still mean "something is wrong", so the synchronous-request SHAPE is the real defect. Filed as **W10-58** (make it a job: return a run id immediately, append phase events, persist partial progress). W10-57 is NOT marked done: its acceptance also requires per-provider-entry configurability, which needs a field on `ProviderEntry` in the registry — outside this ticket's `write_scope` — and the end-to-end run still does not complete. 2026-08-03 W10-65 done, W10-66 done, W10-67 filed — **four distinct failures in the same three-phase pipeline, each only visible once the previous was fixed.** W10-65: one bounded retry with the validation gap fed back. Before it, a phase was a single shot, and a local model that returned well-formed JSON of the right general shape but left `openQuestions[0].slate.recommendedId` empty discarded the whole run. Decision recorded per its acceptance — feed the gap back, do NOT loosen the schema, and the reason was *verified*: `buildFounderSlate` (founder-slate.ts:42) refuses a `recommendedId` not among its options on purpose, so accepting an empty one at the port would only move the refusal deeper. Exactly one retry, and only for `MalformedModelOutputError` — a transport failure or provider timeout is not retried, because the local default is 300s and re-issuing one would double a wait the user is already holding a tab open for. W10-66: `BLUEPRINT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` asked for `openQuestions[].key` with no format, so the model wrote `"Offline Sync & Conflict Resolution Strategy"` and `OPEN_QUESTION_KEY_RE` refused it — **the prompt was wrong, not the validator**, since the key becomes a bare token inside a `<!-- FOUNDER-DECISION: … -->` sentinel matched by a fail-closed grammar. Fixed in two places on purpose: the prompt now states the rule with a worked example (importing the canonical regex from `@dokima/pipeline`, so there is no second copy to drift), and `parseBlueprintInput` validates at the PORT so a bad key becomes the phase-named error W10-65's retry already handles — a prompt is guidance, a boundary is a contract. 2026-08-03 W10-60 done, W10-68 filed — the model picker's slash bug, and the honest cost of fixing it. `splitModelRef` split on the FIRST slash, so 8 of the 23 models a live LM Studio serves this machine (`qwen/…`, `google/…`, `nvidia/…`, `mlx-community/…`) resolved to a providerId that does not exist: the W10-04 panel offered them and the resolver refused them. **Convention decided** per the ticket's own instruction (acceptance 3): a prefix is a providerId only when it names a REGISTERED provider; otherwise the whole value is the model id and reaches the wire intact. `knownProviderIds` is now a REQUIRED argument to `splitModelRef` rather than an optional convenience, because the ambiguity is not resolvable without it and a default would reintroduce the bug for whoever forgot to pass it. Recognition deliberately uses ALL registered ids, not just enabled ones — otherwise `off-box/model` would stop looking like a disabled provider and start looking like a model name, and get sent to whichever provider happened to be the only enabled one. **The honest cost, surfaced by an EXISTING test rather than by me**: W10-03's "refuses a matrix row naming a provider that is not registered" went red, because `ghost/model` (a typo) and `qwen/qwen3-coder-next` (a real model id) are the same shape and one reading has to win. The vendor reading wins because it is the one users actually hit. That test was documentation of intent, so it was **rewritten to assert the new convention with the trade stated in its body** — not deleted, not quietly re-expected. The regression is bounded (two or more enabled providers is still `ambiguous-provider`; a registered-but-disabled prefix is still `unknown-provider`, both asserted) and real. The ambiguity is in the data model, so **W10-68** removes the string: `provider_id` and `model` as separate columns, with a migration that must resolve pre-existing rows identically. Fixtures proven RED — reverting to the first-slash split fails 6 of 19. 2026-08-03 W10-64 done, W10-70 filed — **a model configured once now applies to the next product you create.** `model-matrix-store.ts`'s own header has always described the project rows as "the project-scope override of the global preset"; only the override existed, so `resolveModelTarget` short-circuited to the env target for every new product (`rows.length === 0`) and FR-F3's "register once, use everywhere" was false for the model even after W10-62 made it true for the provider. The preset lives in `~/.dokima/config.json` under `model_matrix` — the same file and mechanism the `providers` key already uses, so there is one global-settings story rather than two, and deliberately NOT a second SQLite table (its own migration, its own single-writer discipline under C-6, and a third place for the CLI and GUI to disagree). Precedence is project-then-global with **no row-by-row merge**: a project with any rows of its own ignores the preset entirely, because a half-merged matrix where a role you never configured here silently comes from elsewhere is harder to reason about than a clean override — and `appendModelMatrixChanged`'s diff would report phantom changes for rows the project never had. The write path ships with it (a read-only fallback would be dead code): PUT takes `scope`, absent means project so every existing caller is byte-identical, an unknown scope is a 400 not a silent downgrade, the panel's "Use for every project" is off by default because a global write is the wider blast radius, and GET reports the winning scope so the panel can say "these rows come from your every-project defaults" — an inherited row is otherwise indistinguishable from a local one and editing it silently creates an override. **PROVEN IN A BROWSER**: the panel wrote the preset, and a SECOND product (Groceries) with zero rows of its own resolved `scope: "global"` with both task types on `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`, showing the inherited notice. **NOT PROVEN, and filed rather than graded**: acceptance 4 asks for a RUN on that second product to use the configured model, and the provider registry still has no global WRITE path — the inheriting product shows "No providers yet", so `resolveModelTarget` takes its `providers.length === 0` branch and falls back to env. That is **W10-70**, a close copy of the shape this ticket just established. **Two defects in my own work, both found by driving it and both fixed here**: the global store had no `(role, taskType)` uniqueness, so the panel's `[...existing, draft]` POST duplicated a row — the SQL table's PRIMARY KEY had been collapsing that silently — and making `listModelMatrix` read global settings made every test in `model-matrix-store.test.ts` depend on the developer's real `~/.dokima`, turning "starts empty for a fresh project" red on this machine. **A scope=global route test also wrote my REAL `~/.dokima/config.json`** before any guard existed, which would have pointed every project on this machine at a fixture model; the file was removed and `DOKIMA_HOME` is now pinned in both harnesses with the reason in a comment. `write_scope` was corrected four times, all recorded on the ticket — twice for the same mistake, scoping a source file without its sibling test, which is a mechanical rule to apply when filing rather than discover when claiming. Fixtures proven RED — removing the fallback fails 3 of 20. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **420 files / 3218 passed | 1 skipped** (+16), **61 e2e passed**.

2026-08-03 W10-67 done — **a run paused on a founder decision now keeps the slates, and resumes without re-running the blueprint.** The gate was always right: FR-P7 locks phase 4 while a marker is unresolved, and `runPreflight` checks it immediately after the blueprint, before any further gateway spend. What was wrong is that the run then threw everything away — measured in a browser on a real idea: two named questions, `decisions 0`, `events 0`, `plan_items 0`. The two existing self-attest red fixtures went red and caught it. Presenting a forgery as "awaiting your decision" would invite the founder to answer a question that does not exist and would soften a Law 4 refusal into a workflow state; only an unresolved-marker refusal pauses now, malformed and forged stay a hard 422, and a new fixture names the distinction. **Resume without re-running the blueprint**: only the blueprint INPUT is persisted, because synthesis is pure and the model call that produced the input is the expensive part — the slate and drafts are deliberately not stored, since the gate fires before either is requested. The acceptance fixture asserts the resume spends exactly 2 gateway calls, not 3. **Law 4 holds on the resume path**: markers are flipped with D-ids `decideSlate` wrote to `docs/DECISIONS.md`, using the imported `resolveOpenQuestion` (never reimplemented), and the gate is RE-RUN against the real on-disk ledger — a resume trusting its own revision would be the self-attestation the run route's header exists to refuse. Paused runs live in `<project>/.dokima/runs/<id>.json` rather than a new event kind, which would mean editing `packages/events` (outside scope); the slates — the half a human answers — go through the real event-logged store. Run ids are validated before touching the filesystem, and a traversal-shaped id is a 404, asserted. Fixtures proven RED — restoring the throwing gate fails 5 of 8. **NOT yet verified in a browser**: the route fixtures drive the real gate, the real decisions store and the real ledger write, but the end-to-end pass through the Decisions UI is unproven and should be the next session's first act. 2026-08-04 W10-74 done; W10-75/76/77 filed — **an end-to-end pass through the product, and the wall it hit.** Driven in a browser at the user's request. WHAT WORKS, and it is more than expected: the ticket lifecycle is real from the founder's seat, and its trust boundary holds. `claim` and `start` move a card from the board's "Move to…" control; `close` is REFUSED with `MANIFEST_INVALID — requires >=1 file, >=1 commit, and verify exit 0 (got files=0, commits=0, verifyExit=1)`, rendered as a dismissible banner with the optimistic move rolled back. **You cannot click a ticket to Done.** At the CLI the rest holds too: `NOT_OWNER` for a non-owner close, `SELF_ACCEPT` for the maker (C-4), refusals exiting 1, a legitimate close minting a receipt the drawer renders with verify/files/commits and a four-step history naming the distinct reviewer, `accept` reaching Done, dependants reflowing to Ready, and `verify-chain` reporting chain OK. **THE WALL: nothing an installed user has could execute any of it.** `dokima run` answered `unknown command 'run'`, and so did `board`, `claim`, `close`, `accept` and `verify-chain` — while `apps/server/src/cli/index.ts` implemented every one of them and closed a real ticket when driven through `tsx`. Three lines of packaging: root `package.json`'s `bin` points at `bootstrap/cli-entry.mjs`, `apps/server/package.json` declares no bin at all, and `build.mjs` has exactly ONE entryPoint, so `cli/index.ts` was never compiled into the tarball. Fixed as **W10-74** by folding `runCli` into `bootstrap/cli.ts` — esbuild follows the import, so no second bin and no second entryPoint, one dispatch and one help text. **Reachability alone would not have been the fix**: the verbs took `--db <path>` while `run` took `--project <id>`, and someone who made a product through the Fleet has the id, not the path to `<project>/.dokima/state.db`. Every verb now accepts `--project <id>` resolved through `~/.dokima/fleet.json`, with `--db` kept as the explicit escape hatch that still wins when both are given, and an unregistered id refused with a message naming where real ids live. Proven with the PACKAGED binary from an unrelated cwd: the whole lifecycle, by project id, ending `chain OK`. **My own fixture was wrong before the code was**: it asserted a self-accept from `in_progress` would be `SELF_ACCEPT`, but the transition guard fires first and correctly says `INVALID_TRANSITION` — accept is legal only from `in_review`. Rewritten to walk the real path, which made it stronger: it now closes with a manifest through the packaged CLI and asserts the ticket reaches `done` for a distinct reviewer, rather than only that the command was found. Fixtures proven RED — 2 of 2 against the shipped code, both on `unknown command 'board'`. **THREE FINDINGS FILED, none folded in quietly.** **W10-77** is the honest headline: `run start --mode new_product` returns in under a second with `run-<ts> started -> running`, and twenty seconds later the board is unchanged, the log has no new events and no berth process exists — `createRun` is bookkeeping and ONLY `--mode onboard` goes on to dispatch. `--berths` is recorded and ignored. **So a human can drive the board end to end and every gate holds; no agent can** — and note precisely what that does and does not mean: the `files`/`commits`/`verify` in the close manifest were supplied BY HAND. `closeTicket` refuses without them and cannot tell who computed them, which is exactly the right test of the trust boundary, but it means "a human can drive the board and the gates hold", not "work was verified". W10-77 is where the manifest starts coming from something that actually ran, and `scripts/conductor.mjs` is not evidence otherwise — it drives THIS repo with `claude -p` and is not the product's harbormaster. **W10-75**: the board is a live projection of HTTP verb calls, not of the event log — `wsHub.publish` lives inside the verb handler, so a CLI close left the open Canvas showing In Progress until a reload. Every change worth watching is made by something other than the founder's browser. **W10-76**: the decomposer accepts prose where `write_scope` requires globs — two boards minutes apart, same prompt and model, one with real globs and one with **8 of 8** tickets carrying acceptance prose in `writeScope` and the verify command in `acceptance`; no validator objects, and prose makes the linter QUIETER because `globOverlaps` matches nothing. That is law 1's enforcement boundary. 2026-08-04 W10-76 done — **a write_scope that is not a path is now a named violation, and the premise I filed it with was wrong.** Two boards built minutes apart by the same model from the same prompt: one carried real globs, the other carried the acceptance criteria in prose on **8 of 8** tickets while the verify command sat in `acceptance`. Nothing objected, because every existing plan-linter check is about seams BETWEEN tickets and none asked whether a scope was path-*shaped*. **THE CORRECTION, made before any code was written**: the ticket claimed a prose scope failed "in the direction of permitting rather than refusing". It is the opposite. `globToRegExp` only treats `*` and `?` specially, so a sentence compiles to `^…the whole sentence…$` and matches no path; `detectScopeViolations` returns every changed path matching NO glob, so an agent working such a ticket has **every** file it touches flagged out-of-scope and the close gate refuses. Fail-CLOSED. The honest severity is therefore an *unworkable* board rather than an unsafe one — and it fails after the model spend, not before it. Verified by reading `globToRegExp` and `detectScopeViolations` rather than repeating myself, and the acceptance clause was rewritten to say so. **Replayed against the board that produced the ticket**, which is the check that matters: all **24 of 24** write_scope entries across forkfest2's 8 tickets are flagged by the new rule — where previously that board reported ZERO violations and the correct board reported five, because `findMissingPackageJsonScope` fires only when `globOverlaps` fails to cover a package.json and prose matches nothing. Wrong input produced a cleaner report; a fixture now pins that it cannot. **A violation, not a refusal**, decided and recorded: `decompose` already surfaces violations with the DAG, and discarding a whole multi-call pipeline over one malformed field is the mistake W10-65 exists to remember. The rule is two-part and fail-safe — whitespace is the unambiguous tell (no write_scope this repo has ever used contains a space), and a bare word with no separator, glob or extension is refused because it could only match one file of exactly that name — with a no-false-positive case pinning the shapes actually in use. **The prompt got the W10-66 treatment**, since this is the same class of defect one field over: it described `"writeScope": string[]` and stopped, so a model filled the field beside `"acceptance"` with acceptance criteria. It now says the field is FILE PATHS AND GLOBS, never prose, with worked examples, and says where the criteria and the verify command each belong. Fixtures proven RED — 3 of 4; the fourth is the no-false-positive guard that must pass both ways. 2026-08-04 W11-01 done — **the Provider contract can finally send tools, closing the asymmetry that forced the only shipped session runner to shell out.** `FinishReason` has carried `'tool_calls'` since the contract was first written, but `ChatRequest` had no `tools` field — the response side was designed for tool calling and the request side never built (FR-G9, D-023). `ChatRequest.tools?: ToolSchema[]` and `ChatResponse.toolCalls?: ToolCall[]` land on the contract, normalized the same way `NormalizedUsage` already normalizes tokens and cost: every adapter that implements tool calling reports the same shape regardless of provider wire format. **oai-compat.ts is the reference implementation** (D-023, local-first): serializes `tools` onto the wire for both `chat()` and `chatStream()`, and parses `tool_calls` back for both the non-streaming response and the streamed case (deltas accumulated by index, id/name landing on the first delta and arguments arriving fragmented across chunks) — a provider that cannot express tool calls must refuse the field, never silently drop it, and leaving `chatStream()` half-done would have meant `finishReason: 'tool_calls'` with no `toolCalls` populated, exactly that silent-drop failure. Malformed tool-call-arguments JSON throws `ProviderResponseShapeError` rather than handing a caller a string to re-decode per-provider. `apps/web/e2e/fixtures/fake-model-gateway.ts` learns the same wire (`ScriptedTurn.toolCalls` → `tool_calls` + `finish_reason: 'tool_calls'`), since it is what every e2e that drives a model goes through and the next ticket (the tool LOOP, W11-02+) needs its own fixture. **RED FIXTURES, both halves**: a request carrying tools serializes them onto the wire; a scripted `tool_calls` response parses into normalized `toolCalls` with `finishReason: 'tool_calls'` — failing before this ticket, where `tools` could not even be expressed on the type. **PROVEN AGAINST A REAL LOCAL MODEL** (law 9 keeps CI on the fake): `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` through LM Studio, given a trivial `get_weather` schema, returned `finishReason: 'tool_calls'` and `toolCalls: [{id: '884480280', name: 'get_weather', arguments: {location: 'NYC'}}]` — a wire format only the fake gateway honoured would have passed this ticket and failed the next one. **The file-size gate caught a real scope gap, and self-widening write_scope was the wrong fix**: the complete implementation pushed `oai-compat.ts` past the repo-wide 400-line cap (W10-49) — the two chapter files that exist for exactly this content, `oai-compat-types.ts` (wire shapes) and `oai-compat-helpers.ts` (normalization, already home to `normalizeFinishReason`), were not in the ticket's `write_scope`. An earlier pass in this session widened `write_scope` to the two chapter files and relocated the wire types/helpers there; the conductor correctly rejected that as an out-of-scope edit — a ticket cannot expand its own `write_scope` unilaterally, no matter how mechanical the relocation. Both chapter files are back to byte-identical with `main`. The fix instead: `RawToolCall`/`RawToolCallDelta`/the streaming delta accumulator/`applyToolCallDelta`/`finalizeToolCallDeltas` moved into `providers/types.ts` (already in `write_scope`, already the home of the public `ToolSchema`/`ToolCall` contract, and a defensible landing spot since these are the OpenAI tool-calling wire shapes shared by both `oai-compat.ts` and `openai.ts`, which delegates to it) — `toRawTool`/`normalizeToolCalls` stayed local to `oai-compat.ts` since only they need `ProviderResponseShapeError`. `oai-compat.ts` lands at 398 lines. `normalizeToolCalls`/`applyToolCallDelta`/`finalizeToolCallDeltas` are verbatim moves from the earlier pass; `toRawTool` was re-expressed via destructuring to fit the cap but emits the same JSON (asserted by the existing request-serialization tests) — so the LM Studio evidence above still holds. **Recorded gap, not fixed here**: `ChatMessage` still cannot express a tool *result* (`role: 'tool'` + a call id to answer), so the loop ticket cannot round-trip a tool response yet — adding it would touch `anthropic.ts`/`openai.ts`/`copilot-chat.ts`, all outside this scope. Laws 1–6, unchanged, carry what's permanently forbidden regardless of what's declared). **Caught and reverted my own false statement before it shipped**: a first draft explained `memory`/`forge`/`pipeline`'s empty rows as "currently unimplemented" — false, grep confirms all three ship real tested code (`memory/src/store`, `memory/src/packer`, `pipeline/src/challenger`, `forge/src/github-status.ts`); both already matched real imports exactly. **HANDOFF (schema flag, not this ticket's to fix)**: W11-02's `write_scope` is only `packages/harbormaster/src/agent-session/**` and `src/index.ts` — it does not cover `packages/harbormaster/package.json` or `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, so when it adds `@dokima/mcp` it cannot also satisfy P10 without a `write_scope` widening; flagged in the ARCHITECTURE.md agent-session row instead of asserting a promise that ticket cannot structurally keep. 2026-08-05 W11-06 done — **`chatStream()` no longer silently drops `tools` — the exact thing FR-G9 says an adapter must never do, and the same wave that shipped FR-G9 (W11-01) broke it.** `openai.ts`'s `streamEvents()` is a hand-duplicated copy of the OpenAI wire format, predating W11-01, which that ticket never touched: it never serialized `request.tools` onto the wire and never parsed `choice.delta.tool_calls` off it, so a `chatStream()` call carrying tools returned a normal stream and the caller never learned the field was ignored — worse than unmet, because the contract now claimed a guarantee the adapter didn't keep. **Full delegation to `this.inner.chatStream()` (the `OaiCompatProvider` instance already held for every non-streaming method) was the pre-filed scope note's preferred fix and was tried first, then rejected on evidence, not preference**: `OaiCompatProvider.chatStream()` awaits `ensureWarm()` — an unconditional `GET /models` — and acquires its own, second `RequestQueue` slot *before* its SSE loop starts. Delegating would have added a network round-trip to every cloud stream and moved the queue-acquisition point, which breaks `openai.test.ts`'s concurrency-queue test at the exact microtask boundary it pins (`queueStats().active` would read 0, not 1, while the warm-up ping is in flight) — a product behavior change riding on a tool-calling bugfix, not something this ticket asked for. **The chosen fix instead**: `toRawTool`/`normalizeToolCalls` land as new exported functions in `providers/types.ts`, joining the pre-existing `applyToolCallDelta`/`finalizeToolCallDeltas` — that file's own header comment already declared itself the shared FR-G9 home for exactly this reason. `openai.ts`'s `streamEvents()` imports and uses them directly, keeping its own transport/timing (no warm-up, no second queue, zero behavior change to existing callers) while actually serializing and parsing tools like `oai-compat.ts` does. `oai-compat.ts` keeps its own private copies untouched (outside this ticket's `write_scope`); a future ticket that owns that file can finish the consolidation the W11-01 STATUS entry already flagged as unfinished. **The misleading header comment is rewritten, not left to half-match**: `types.ts` claimed openai.ts's cloud path "delegates to" `oai-compat.ts`, true only for non-streaming `Provider` methods — the new comment says so explicitly, and names the warm-up/second-queue reason `chatStream()` doesn't, so the next reader doesn't have to re-derive it. **Not reachable from production today**, confirmed rather than assumed: `gateway-model-port/provider.ts` still refuses to construct the `openai` kind, so this was live public API (`createOpenAiProvider`/`chatStream`) with no runtime caller, exercised only by `openai.test.ts` — exactly what the ticket said. Fixtures proven RED — 3 of 3 (tool serialization onto the streaming wire, tool_calls delta accumulation into normalized `toolCalls`, and `chat()`'s internal `stream` toggle sharing the same fix via `streamEvents()`) failed with "expected undefined to deeply equal [...]" against the unfixed adapter, captured verbatim before the fix; all pass now, plus a 4th (tools omitted from the wire when the request carries none) added for parity with the non-streaming path.
