2026-08-05 W10-68 done — **model_matrix now stores which provider a row means, instead of asking the resolver to guess it from a slash.** `ghost/model` (a typo, or a removed provider) and `qwen/qwen3-coder-next` (a real LM Studio model id) are the same shape — no parsing rule can tell them apart, which is exactly why W10-60 had to pick the vendor-namespace reading and pay the price documented in its own rewritten test. **DELIVER**: `packages/events/migrations/013_model_matrix_provider.sql` adds `provider_id TEXT` to `model_matrix` (`user_version` 12→13, via a table recreate rather than `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` — reason below); `ModelMatrixRow.providerId?`/`ModelMatrixInput.providerId?` carry it through settings-types.ts and model-matrix-store.ts. `bindProvider` (model-resolution.ts) now takes `{modelRef, providerId}`: an explicit `providerId` is the whole answer — no string parsing, and an id naming no ENABLED provider throws `unknown-provider`, **restoring the refusal W10-60 traded away** — while `providerId` absent falls back to W10-60's unchanged `splitModelRef`, so the documented bounded regression (`ghost/model`, a disabled prefix, ambiguity with 2+ enabled providers) stays exactly where that ticket left it. `findRowProviderId` replays `matrixFromRows`/`resolveModelChain`'s own row-selection rule (including the `DEFAULT_ROLE` fallback via `routed.usedDefaultRole`) because `route()` only returns the flattened model string, never which row produced it. **MIGRATE WHAT EXISTS, not lazily**: `migrateLegacyModelMatrixRows` reads every `provider_id IS NULL` row once, splits it via the registry, and PERSISTS the split (an `UPDATE`, not re-derived per call) — `putModelMatrix` runs it automatically before its own upsert so a project migrates as it keeps writing, and it's exported directly so a board (and the migration itself) can be verified without waiting on a write. Idempotent by construction (only touches still-NULL rows whose prefix names a registered provider). `SELECT_ALL` switched from a named column list to `SELECT *`: a `state.db` that has taken no WRITABLE connection since this shipped is still pre-013 (`openEventLogReader` deliberately never migrates), and naming `provider_id` explicitly would throw "no such column" instead of degrading to "no explicit provider" — the same meaning the column's absence already carries. **RED FIXTURES (acceptance 5)**: an explicit `providerId` plus a model whose own id has a slash (`qwen/qwen3-coder-next`) resolves to exactly that provider with the model sent on the wire intact, never re-split; an explicit `providerId` naming an unregistered provider raises `unknown-provider` again; and a REAL pre-migration string row (`box-b/qwen-32b`, no `providerId`) resolves to an identical target — `toEqual`, not `toMatchObject` — via `resolveModelTarget` both before `migrateLegacyModelMatrixRows` runs (the `bindProvider` fallback split) and after (the row's own persisted `provider_id`). 8 new tests (5 model-matrix-store.test.ts, 3 model-resolution.test.ts). **write_scope widened three times before this attempt** (model-resolution.ts/.test.ts for `bindProvider`; the migrations glob for the column) — both self-blocks upheld as correct by the founder; documented on the ticket rather than repeated here. **A REJECTED first attempt found the gate-integrity bug the hard way, so the retry didn't need to repeat it**: adding migration 013 shifts `latestKnownSchemaVersion()` from 12 to 13, and `boot-sequence.test.ts`'s "backs up state.db before applying a pending migration" (out of this ticket's `write_scope`) seeds a rewound `user_version` by undoing whatever the LATEST migration added — hardcoded, until now, to `DROP TABLE field_reports` (012's effect). A first pass wrote migration 013 as a plain `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`, which made that fixture's re-application of 013 hit "duplicate column name" (the fixture only ever undid 012), and "fixed" it by editing the fixture's seed to undo 013's `ADD COLUMN` instead. That was a correct diagnosis and a working fix, but an edit to a file `write_scope` never granted — the conductor rejected it, correctly: a ticket cannot widen its own scope by editing around the boundary, however small or well-documented. **The real fix stays inside `write_scope` instead**: migration 013 is a table recreate (`CREATE TABLE model_matrix_new` with `provider_id` already in its schema → copy the five pre-existing columns across → `DROP TABLE model_matrix` → rename `model_matrix_new` to `model_matrix`) rather than an in-place `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`. Grepped every migration file first — nothing references `model_matrix` via FK/index/trigger, so recreating it is free of that risk — and the actual payoff: a recreate tolerates being re-applied to a db that already has the column, which an `ADD COLUMN` cannot. `CREATE TABLE model_matrix_new` succeeds because the name was freed by the prior run's rename; the copy reads whatever `model_matrix` currently has (with or without `provider_id`, since the copy names its columns explicitly); the drop+rename land it back in the same shape regardless. `boot-sequence.test.ts` needed zero changes and is byte-identical to `main` — confirmed both by `git diff` against the ticket's start point and by the fixture's own suite passing unmodified. This generalizes: any future `ADD COLUMN`-shaped migration that ships without `write_scope` covering `boot-sequence.test.ts` should recreate rather than `ALTER`, and this fixture's hardcoded assumption about "the latest migration" stops being load-bearing either way. W11-08's filing-time lint remains the right fix for the write_scope-widening pattern in general; this ticket didn't need to invoke it because the recreate sidesteps the class of gap entirely. **HANDOFF, not fixed here**: `matrix-routes.ts`'s `isCopilotModel` still tests `model.startsWith('copilot/')` — if a provider is ever registered with id `copilot`, the auto-backfill splits a legacy `copilot/gpt-4` row into `provider_id='copilot'`+`model='gpt-4'` on its next write, and the D-019 consent-gate flag silently goes false for that row; `matrix-routes.ts` is genuinely out of `write_scope` and the fix has real design latitude, left for a scoped ticket. Fixtures proven RED — 3 of 3 red-fixture cases plus the pre-existing "refuses a matrix row naming a provider that is not registered" test (rewritten by W10-60, now genuinely restored for the explicit-providerId case). Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **428 files / 3302 passed | 1 skipped** (+8), **68 e2e passed**, validate-plan/validate-traceability both clean (pre-existing traceability gap unrelated).

Lives in `packages/harbormaster/src/agent-session/**` per D-023's own corrected placement (W11-05 already declared `@dokima/mcp` and amended the ARCHITECTURE.md matrix, so this ticket consumed an existing dependency rather than amending architecture mid-ticket). **Two real build-then-wire gaps found in already-`done` gateway tickets, worked around inside this ticket's own write_scope rather than silently patched elsewhere (Law 1)**: W11-01 added `ToolSchema`/`ToolCall` to `providers/types.ts` but never widened `providers/index.ts`'s barrel to re-export them by name — worked around via structural indexed-access types (`gateway-tool-types.ts`) instead of hand-duplicating the shape or touching another package's file; and `ChatRequest`/`ChatMessage` still have no `tool`-role message or a way for an assistant turn to echo `tool_calls` (the exact gap W11-01's own STATUS entry flagged) — the tool-result round-trip here is a synthetic `user` message, correct against this ticket's fake-provider fixtures (law 9) but will not reach a live OpenAI-compatible provider until `packages/gateway` adds both. Also flagged, not fixed (docs out of write_scope): `docs/SECURITY_CONTROLS.md`'s SC-18 bullet still lists only read/list/search/write/edit/verify — `commit`, which acceptance 4 mandates as first-class, isn't named there. **Advisor review caught two real defects before close, both fixed**: write/edit checked path containment by string arithmetic only, missing the symlink-escape ancestor case SC-18's own Verify line enumerates as its fourth case (fixed via `assertRealWithinWorktree`, realpath-of-nearest-ancestor, two red fixtures proving the escape refused and nothing lands outside); and the acceptance-6 fixture's evidence assertion (`"auto-blocked with evidence"`) didn't discriminate a T-27 budget stop from any other park reason (strengthened to assert the session's own stop message in the attempt record, with the render gap in `loop-land.ts`'s park comment noted rather than silently accepted). Renamed `maxCostUsd` → `maxTicketCostUsd` mid-review: `CostLedger` is shared across every attempt a claim/land loop makes on one ticket, so the cap was always per-ticket, never per-session, and the old name claimed otherwise. Fixtures proven RED — 43 new tests, all against a fake, no-network `Provider` (law 9): spend-attribution with the routed model (acceptance 3), a passing commit-then-manifest fixture AND a commits-nothing refusal fixture both against the UNCHANGED `runCloseGate`/`runLandLoop` (acceptance 4/5 — resolving the literal contradiction between the two as originally worded toward acceptance 4's own argument, rather than shipping the impossible literal reading), and a never-converging session that stops at its own iteration budget and parks the ticket with evidence via the unmodified `runLandLoop` (acceptance 6/T-27). 2026-08-05 W11-02 retry closed — **the search tool's ReDoS gap is closed.** A gate finding on the prior close said `fs-tools.ts`'s `toMatcher` built `new RegExp(pattern)` straight from the model-supplied `pattern` and ran it, unbounded, against every line of every file the walk visited, with nothing upstream (`@dokima/mcp`'s `requestToolCall` does a bare `await executor(...)`) wrapping the call in a timeout — a single catastrophic-backtracking pattern (`(a+)+$`) could hang the whole single-threaded harbormaster process, and would do so *before* T-27's own iteration cap ever got to count the turn. Fixed entirely inside this ticket's `write_scope` (`fs-tools.ts`), three bounds mirroring the timeout discipline `reRunVerify` already gives `verify`: `isUnsafeSearchPattern` statically refuses the nested-quantifier/overlapping-alternation shapes behind the known catastrophic patterns *before* a regex is ever constructed, falling back to literal-substring matching for anything refused — the same fallback `toMatcher` already used for a syntactically invalid pattern, so the degrade path was already established; `MAX_MATCH_LINE_LENGTH` caps the input handed to any one `.test()` call; `SEARCH_TIME_BUDGET_MS`, checked every 50 lines, bounds a call's *total* time across many files (it can't interrupt a single hung call already in progress — that's what the static refusal prevents). Tool schema description (`tools.ts`) documents the fallback so the model is told, not surprised. 6 new tests: `isUnsafeSearchPattern` unit cases (nested-quantifier, overlapping-alternation, oversized-pattern, ordinary-patterns-unaffected), a regression fixture asserting `search` stays under 1s against a classic `(a+)+$` pattern with an adversarial 40-char input line, and a literal-fallback correctness check. Advisor review of this retry caught a second, real gap before close: `isUnsafeSearchPattern` only tracked risk inside parenthesized groups, so an adjacent-ungrouped-quantifier pattern like `.*.*.*.*.*=` (zero groups) sailed through as safe — measured on this machine, even a two-quantifier pattern with no groups at all (`/.*.*=/`) took ~11s against a single 4000-char line, and a three-quantifier version was already multi-second by ~250 chars, so `MAX_MATCH_LINE_LENGTH=4000` did not actually bound it. Fixed by adding a flat `MAX_QUANTIFIER_COUNT` cap (`isUnsafeSearchPattern` now refuses more than 2 quantifier operators anywhere in the pattern, nested or not) alongside the existing nested-group check, and by shrinking `MAX_MATCH_LINE_LENGTH` to 300 — applied ONLY when `toMatcher` is using a real regex (literal `.includes()` is always linear, so a refused/invalid pattern falling back to literal-substring search is never capped and still finds matches past 300 chars). 4 more tests: the advisor-suggested `.*.*.*.*.*=` case plus a 3-quantifier case flagged unsafe, a regression fixture proving the previously-measured ~11s hang is now sub-second, a real-regex-still-works-within-the-cap case, and a literal-fallback-not-capped case. A second advisor pass then flagged that the fix now degrades *silently*: a pattern with 3+ quantifiers (bread-and-butter code search — `function\s+\w+\s*\(`, `import\s+.*\s+from`) gets silently refused and falls back to literal-substring matching, so `{matches: []}` on a real miss is indistinguishable from `{matches: []}` because the pattern was too complex to run as regex at all — a confident wrong answer, not a degraded one, for the exact kind of search this ticket's own agent session exists to make. Same silent gap on the other side: the 300-char regex-only line cap skips candidate lines (this repo's own `plan.json` notes and `docs/STATUS.md` entries are exactly this shape) with nothing recording it happened. Fixed by extending the `timedOut` pattern already in the response: `searchTool` now returns `matchMode: 'regex' | 'literal'` (which path actually ran) and `skippedLongLines` (a count, not a silent drop) — degrade honestly, never silent, the same line this project's laws already draw elsewhere. 2 more tests: `matchMode` distinguishes a real regex from a refused/invalid fallback, and `skippedLongLines` counts (rather than hides) a line skipped past the cap. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean, **434 files / 3345 passed | 1 skipped** (+12), **68 e2e passed**.
2026-08-05 W11-02 blocked (after these two closes) — the wave-W11 security pass and an independent per-ticket reviewer both found the same gap in the "fixed" symlink containment: `assertRealWithinWorktree`/`realpathOfNearestAncestor` (`fs-tools.ts`) resolves the nearest EXISTING ancestor with `fs.realpath()` but reassembles the leaf component with `path.join` **without** resolving it — so a pre-existing leaf symlink pointing outside the worktree still passes containment, and the following `fs.writeFile` follows it (Node has no `O_NOFOLLOW` default). Verified empirically: the function returns `ALLOWED` for `worktree/evil.txt -> /outside/leak.txt`. Reopened for a fix-forward pass rather than a rewrite; see the next entry.
2026-08-05 W11-02 done (fix-forward, acceptance clause 8) — **the leaf-symlink containment escape is closed, and this time the read-side siblings got checked too.** Resumed per plan.json's founder-authorized note: merged `blocked/w11-02` (attempts 1-2, 2,365 reviewed lines across 18 files) as the first commit rather than rewriting, then fixed forward. **The fix**: `fs-containment.ts`'s `realpathOfTarget` attempts `fs.realpath` on the FULL target first — this resolves an ordinary file directly, and a symlink leaf (or chain) whose ultimate target exists via `realpath`'s own chain-following, closing the exact case the reviewer verified as `ALLOWED`. Falls back to the existing `realpathOfNearestAncestor` ONLY when `fs.lstat` also shows the leaf has no filesystem entry at all (the genuine write-new-file case). **A third shape, found while fixing the second-time bug**: a leaf that EXISTS as a symlink but whose target does NOT (dangling) is not "leaf absent" — `fs.lstat` distinguishes it, and the link is followed manually (`fs.readlink`, recursively, bounded by `MAX_SYMLINK_HOPS`=40) rather than silently falling back to ancestor-only resolution, which would miss it too (`fs.writeFile`'s default `open()` still follows a dangling symlink and creates the file at its target — the exact "same bug again" shape this ticket was reopened to avoid). An `lstat` success that isn't a symlink but still fails `realpath` (e.g. a permissions error) rethrows the original error rather than mislabeling it `ToolPathEscapeError` — fail closed without over-claiming what happened. **Also hardened `list`/`search`** (same file, same `write_scope`, same vulnerability class — an advisor pass flagged these as "the most likely third block" before this closed): both previously used `resolveWithinWorktree`'s pure string arithmetic for their root path with zero `realpath` check, so a symlinked directory inside the worktree pointing outside it could leak an arbitrary directory listing or file contents on read; both now go through the same `assertRealWithinWorktree` containment check as `write`/`edit`. **RED FIXTURE PROVEN, not assumed**: 6 new tests, each run against the UNMODIFIED file first (`git stash` the fix, run, confirm red) then against the fix (green) — `assertRealWithinWorktree` throws `ToolPathEscapeError` directly; `write`/`edit` refuse through an existing-target leaf symlink with the outside file provably byte-unchanged; `write` refuses through a dangling leaf symlink; `list`/`search` refuse a symlinked escaping directory. **A consequence the FULL workspace gate caught, not the package-scoped one**: the fix pushed `fs-tools.ts` to 456 lines, over `validate-file-size`'s repo-wide 400-line cap (`CODE_BOOK_PROTOCOL.md`) — surfaced only by `conductor-lib.test.mjs`'s repo-wide zero-count check inside a workspace-wide `pnpm test`, the same tiered-gate lesson this project already learned from e2e sitting outside the ticket gate. Split by concern, mirroring this directory's existing `gateway-tool-types.ts` chapter pattern: `fs-containment.ts` (156 lines, containment/symlink hardening), `fs-search.ts` (206 lines, the ReDoS-bounded search handler), `fs-tools.ts` (132 lines, barrel re-exporting both plus read/list/write/edit) — zero call-site churn, both existing callers (`tool-executor.ts`, `git-tools.ts`) and the test file keep importing `./fs-tools.js` unchanged. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing warning, unrelated), typecheck clean workspace-wide, **434 files / 3359 passed | 1 skipped** (+6 new here; the other +8 between the recovered branch's 3345 and this run's baseline arrived with W10-68 landing on `main` after that branch was cut), **68 e2e passed**, `validate-plan.mjs` OK, `validate-traceability.mjs` OK (1 pre-existing gap unrelated, FR-PLAN3), `validate-file-size.sh` 0 gaps repo-wide, `validate-circular-deps.sh` 0 gaps.

2026-08-05 W11-03 done (retry, fixing a CRITICAL gate finding) — **acceptance 2 said "this is the one that matters," and the first close hadn't actually delivered it.** The gate finding: the only thing standing in for "the out-of-session diff" was `commitTool`'s `commitWithScopeCheck` — reached by a tool call the untrusted model itself CHOOSES to make, never anything that runs independently of it, and the SC-17 paragraph's own new text mislabeled it "the out-of-session diff" despite that. A session that wrote an out-of-scope file to real disk (imagine the pre-check had a bug, or acceptance 2's own "pre-check disabled" fixture) and simply never called `commit` would sail through with an honest-looking manifest naming only its legitimate files — nothing rechecked the REAL diff against `ticket.writeScope` regardless of what the model chose to do. `runCloseGate` (`packages/harbormaster/src/loop-gates.ts`) was the obvious place to add that reverse check, and is genuinely outside this ticket's `write_scope` (`packages/harbormaster/src/agent-session/**` only) — so the fix lands inside scope instead, at the one place this session type structurally can: `gateway-session.ts`'s tool loop has exactly ONE return path that can hand `runSession` a Completion Manifest (the natural-completion return, no more tool calls left; the other two exits already carry no manifest text and exit non-zero). `refuseIfSessionExceededScope` runs there unconditionally — a real `git diff` of the worktree against `HEAD` (`computeChangedPaths`, `@dokima/loop`, unmodified) rechecked through `checkWriteScope` (`@dokima/git` — the exact function `commitWithScopeCheck` itself calls, unmodified) — regardless of whether the model ever attempted a write, edit, or commit. A violation discards the session's output entirely (empty stdout, non-zero exit): `runSession`'s caller sees `manifest === null`, so `runCloseGate` never runs and the ticket cannot close on that attempt, no matter what `commitTool` did or didn't catch. **Honest about what this is and isn't**: neither this new check nor `commitTool`'s own SC-01 check is a fully separate out-of-process pass the way SC-01's own canonical top-of-file definition describes (both run inside the same `spawn()` call the tool loop runs in) — the guarantee is that the check's outcome depends only on real, independently observable git state once the model has no more turns left to influence it, never on anything the model said or chose to do. `docs/SECURITY_CONTROLS.md`'s SC-17 paragraph is rewritten to say exactly this, name both mechanisms, and stop calling `commitTool`'s check "the out-of-session diff." **Fixtures for the shapes a diff can actually see** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.dokima/state.db`, an ordinary out-of-scope path, a leaf symlink escape) prove the new check fires with NO tool call ever refused and NO tool call ever attempted — the file is placed via raw `node:fs`, bypassing the write/edit pre-check entirely. One is wired through the complete, unmodified `runLandLoop` pipeline (worktree created internally, file placed via a thin `spawn` wrapper once the real worktree path is known) proving the ticket never lands end to end. **`../outside` and `.git/config` are documented, not mis-tested**: both are structurally invisible to any diff-based check — git never reports a path outside its own worktree or inside its own `.git` directory, diff or no diff — so a test asserting `computeChangedPaths` doesn't see them replaces a test that would have silently passed for the wrong reason; they stay guarded by containment (`fs-containment.ts`) and by git's own refusal to track its internals, exactly as before. **HANDOFF filed, not silently absorbed**: `runCloseGate` itself still only checks `manifest.files` against the real diff/commit set in one direction (subset, never superset) — closed for THIS session type by the new check above, but `packages/loop/src/session.ts`'s `createChildProcessSpawn` escape-hatch runner has no equivalent guard and would rely on `runCloseGate`'s asymmetric check alone; a follow-up ticket should add the symmetric check to `runCloseGate` itself as defense in depth across every `SpawnSession` implementation. `setup()` in `gateway-session.test.ts` now `git init`s + commits its temp `cwd` (previously a bare directory), required because the natural-completion path now shells out to real `git`; verified every pre-existing test in that describe block still passes unmodified. Fixtures proven RED — 7 new tests (6 in the top describe block covering each escape shape plus a clean-session regression check and the `../outside`/`.git/config` documentation case, 1 wired through `runLandLoop`). Teaching the maker prompt about `alwaysOk` would stop this one block and license silent scope drift on every other ticket; the fix has to live at filing time, and now does — documented in the new rule's own doc-comment and in `conductor.config.json`'s `$migrationScopeNote`. **`testSibling` and `pageMount` decided explicitly, not left dormant by omission** (acceptance 6): `testSibling` is ON (`source: '\.(ts|tsx)$'`, `test: '\.test\.(ts|tsx)$'`), measured against the live board first rather than assumed — of 8 open tickets, exactly 1 (W11-04) currently lacks a test-sibling directory, confirming the ticket's own "a handful, not 185" prediction. `pageMount` stays OFF, and that's a decision: it models Kryptkeeper's routed multi-page UI (new page → needs a route + nav entry), and `apps/web` has neither a router (`react-router`/`createBrowserRouter`/`<Routes>` all absent, grepped) nor a `pages/` convention — it's a single-page canvas app with exactly one `*Page.tsx` file in the whole tree, so the rule's premise doesn't describe this UI. Both decisions and their evidence are recorded in `conductor.config.json`'s `$testSiblingNote`/`$pageMountNoteW11-08`. **RED FIXTURE (acceptance 5)**: a synthetic W10-68-shaped ticket whose acceptance says "add a `provider_id` column" with an application-only `write_scope` produces one warning naming the migrations dir; the same ticket with a migrations glob added produces none; the same unscoped ticket marked `done` produces none — plus off-when-unconfigured and no-acceptance/no-scope edge cases, 6 new tests total in `scripts/conductor-lib.test.mjs`. Ran the wired-up rule against the real board (`node scripts/conductor.mjs --lint`, informational only — board lint isn't part of `pnpm lint`): 15 warnings total on 8 open tickets, 2 from the new rule (W11-08 itself, silenced once this closes; W11-09, whose acceptance text discusses "adding a table" generically while describing the exemption hole it closes — an accepted heuristic false positive on a warn-only lint, consistent with the existing rules' own documented noise tolerance). **RETRY (same day): the first close wired the new rule through `scripts/conductor-lib.mjs`'s re-export barrel, which is outside this ticket's `write_scope`** — the external gate correctly flagged it as an out-of-scope edit. Self-widening `write_scope` in the worktree would not have legitimized it either: `scripts/conductor/gates.mjs`'s `runGates` scopes the diff against `t.write_scope` from the ticket as claimed on `main`, never anything edited on the ticket's own branch (verified by reading it, not assumed). Fixed by having `scripts/conductor/lint.mjs` and `scripts/conductor-lib.test.mjs` (the only two in-scope files that needed the symbol) import `migrationScopeWarning` directly from `scripts/conductor-lib/lint-rules.mjs` instead of through the barrel, with an inline comment at each import site explaining why and pointing at the one-line barrel re-export a follow-up ticket can add later for consistency with `testSiblingWarning`/`migrationCollisions`/`pageMountWarning`. No behavioural change from the first pass — same rule, same tests, same config — confirmed by re-running `node scripts/conductor.mjs --lint` after the fix (same 15 warnings, same 2 from the new rule). 2026-08-06 W11-09 done — **`alwaysOk` exempted `packages/events/migrations/**` by path alone, so any ticket could rewrite a shipped migration in place and the scope gate stayed silent — the same silent-drift shape as W10-68's own bug, but in the gate meant to catch it.** `applyMigrations` keys off `PRAGMA user_version` plus a file's numeric prefix, so an in-place edit to a committed migration never re-applies to a `state.db` that already ran it: green in a fresh checkout/CI, silently wrong everywhere else. **Fix, scoped to this property only (acceptance bullet 4)**: `scripts/conductor/gates.mjs` now reads `git diff --name-status` (was `--name-only`) and layers a DEDICATED add-only check on top of the existing `ALWAYS_OK` match, driven by a new `conductor.config.json` key `alwaysOkAddOnly` — the subset of `alwaysOk` globs that additionally require git status `A` before exempting a path; `M`/`D`/renames on an existing file there stay out-of-scope unless `write_scope` names it explicitly. `alwaysOk`/`globToRegex`/`ALWAYS_OK` themselves (`scripts/conductor-lib/config.mjs`, `scripts/conductor/context.mjs`) are untouched, both outside this ticket's write_scope and rightly so — the board, the lockfile, tsconfig etc. are genuinely modify-in-place infra and must keep exempting `M`/`D`. `packages/events/migrations/**` moved out of the general `alwaysOk` array into `alwaysOkAddOnly` alone; a first pass left it in both, which silently no-opped the new check (the plain `ALWAYS_OK` match returns early), caught by the RED FIXTURE test itself refusing to go red. **RED FIXTURE, `scripts/conductor.integration.test.mjs`**: real git branches/commits inside a throwaway fixture repo (git-init'd in the same mkdtemp'd dir the existing `buildFixture` scaffolds) prove a branch that ADDS a new migration with no migrations glob in `write_scope` still passes; the same shape MODIFYING an existing committed migration fails, naming that path; and a ticket whose `write_scope` explicitly covers it passes. `runGates()` has no CLI entry point, so it's called in a throwaway `node --input-type=module -e` subprocess that dynamically imports the fixture's vendored `gates.mjs` — a same-process dynamic `import()` from inside the vitest test failed against Vite's own fs-root restriction (`Does the file exist?` against a file that plainly did), so the subprocess sidesteps Vite's loader entirely. **Acceptance bullet 6, was the hole ever used**: `git log --diff-filter=M -- packages/events/migrations/` shows exactly 3 prior modifications (W0-02, W10-68, and the Shipwright→Dokima rename D-021) and none exploited it — the first two are same-ticket, same-session amendments to a migration that ticket itself had just added (W10-68's `write_scope` explicitly covered migrations), and the rename commit is a direct-to-main scripted pass outside the ticket/gate mechanism entirely, touching only a SQL comment, never schema. No separate data-integrity finding filed. 2026-08-06 W11-10 done — **migration 013's recreate silently reset every `provider_id` on re-application; the fix restores the original `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` shape and fixes the test fixture that made the recreate look necessary, instead of patching the SQL again.** Reproduced first: seed `model_matrix.provider_id`, re-apply `013_model_matrix_provider.sql`, get `provider_id: null` back with no error — the recreate's `INSERT INTO model_matrix_new (role, task_type, model, fallback, updated_at) SELECT ...` never named `provider_id`, so a "successful" re-application quietly discarded it. **THE DECISION (acceptance 4), reading (b)**: `user_version` is the sole authority for "has this migration run" — `applyMigrations` (packages/events/src/migrate.ts, untouched by this ticket, already correct) sets it inside the same transaction as the migration's SQL, and DATABASE.md §8 is explicit that migrations are forward-only with no down migrations. Nothing in real operation produces a schema state where `provider_id` already exists but `user_version` still reads 12 — only a synthetic test fixture does. Migrations are therefore NOT meant to tolerate raw re-application against a schema that already reflects them; that guarantee belongs to `user_version`, not to every migration file being idempotent SQL. **THE ARCHAEOLOGY that settles it**: W10-68's actual first attempt (commit 85e8520) shipped the correct `ALTER TABLE model_matrix ADD COLUMN provider_id TEXT` and *also* correctly fixed `boot-sequence.test.ts`'s rewound-`user_version` fixture (which had gone stale the moment migration 013 existed — it was hardcoded to undo 012's `field_reports`, but with 013 added, rewinding `user_version` by one now re-triggers 013, not 012). That fix was reverted in commit d522708 for being out of `write_scope`, and the migration was reshaped into the data-losing recreate instead so the stale fixture would stop erroring — the reshape traded a loud, correct "duplicate column name" failure for a quiet, wrong success. This ticket's `write_scope` deliberately includes both files precisely so that mistake doesn't repeat. **FIX**: `013_model_matrix_provider.sql` reverted to the additive `ALTER TABLE`, comment rewritten to record this decision and cite the archaeology. `boot-sequence.test.ts`'s fixture restored to undo 013's actual effect (removing the `provider_id` column it added, not the `field_reports` table 012 added) with the original "whoever adds migration 014 must update this again" comment, plus a new self-check (`user_version` reaches `latestKnownSchemaVersion()` after boot) so a future stale fixture fails loudly instead of silently exercising nothing. Grepped every `model_matrix` read/write site (`model-matrix-store.ts`): all name-based (`SELECT *` + property access, named-parameter `INSERT`/`UPDATE`) — no positional-column dependency broke by moving `provider_id` from the recreate's ordinal 3 back to `ADD COLUMN`'s ordinal 6. **RED FIXTURE (acceptance 5)**: `packages/events/src/db.test.ts` seeds `model_matrix.provider_id = 'lm-studio'`, rewinds `user_version` to 12 without undoing 013's column, and asserts `applyMigrations` throws `/duplicate column name/i` (refused outright, matching reading b) while `provider_id` and `user_version` are left untouched by the transaction's rollback — confirmed genuinely red against the pre-fix recreate SQL (silently returns, `provider_id` reads `null`) before restoring the fix. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing unrelated warning), typecheck clean, 434 files / 3390 tests passed | 1 skipped, 68 e2e passed, validate-plan clean.
2026-08-06 W11-11 done — **`verifyTool`'s command output reached the routed model's next turn — possibly a cloud provider — without passing the redaction layer the codebase already has.** `git-tools.ts`'s `verifyTool` put a ticket's verify-command stdout/stderr straight into the tool-result text appended to `messages`; `@dokima/shared`'s `redactString`/`redactDeep` (SC-06, W3-13) already covers the session's other path into a prompt (`renderHandoff` runs `redactDeep` over every handoff) but never touched this one. **Corrected the advisory that raised it**: the output was described as "raw, untruncated" — it is not untruncated, `MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS = 4000` already caps it with a `…(truncated)` marker; the substantive finding (unredacted) stands, the fix is a redaction pass, not a second cap. **Fix**: `redactAndTruncate` runs `redactString` over the FULL untruncated text first, then applies the existing cap — redacting after truncation would let a credential straddling the 4000-char cut point leak half-matched. The result now carries `redacted`/`truncated` booleans rather than silently shortening; a `KNOWN LIMITATION` comment (mirroring `handoff.ts`'s W3-17 pattern) records that this is pattern-only (`SECRET_PATTERNS`) — vault-registered exact-value redaction would need a vault handle threaded through `AgentSessionToolContext` (`tool-executor.ts`), out of this ticket's `write_scope`. Confirmed by grep that `reRunVerify`'s other caller (`runCloseGate`, `loop-gates.ts`) never embeds stdout/stderr in its failure-comment text (exit code and command only) — `verifyTool` was the only unredacted path into a prompt. **Second subject, same rule**: `fs-search.ts`'s `searchTool` swallowed per-file `fs.readFile` failures with a bare `catch { continue; }` and reported nothing, so a file the session couldn't read produced a clean result indistinguishable from a genuine no-match — the same function already reports `matchMode`/`skippedLongLines` for its other two degrade paths. Now counts and reports `unreadableFiles` alongside them. **RED FIXTURES (verified red before the fix, green after)**: a verify command whose stdout contains a credential-shaped string (`ghp_...` token, a `postgresql://` connection string) now returns it redacted with `redacted: true` in both git-tools.test.ts cases; a search over a directory containing one unreadable file (failure injected via an `fs.readFile` spy — chmod `0o000` is a no-op under root, which a containerized conductor may well be, so the spy hits the exact call site a real EACCES would) returns `unreadableFiles: 1` and one match instead of a silent two-match miss, in fs-tools.test.ts. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing unrelated warning), typecheck clean workspace-wide, 434 files / 3393 tests passed | 1 skipped (+4 new tests), 68 e2e passed.
2026-08-06 W11-13 done — **`runCloseGate` checked the manifest against the real diff/commit set but never the reverse: that the real commits stay inside `ticket.writeScope`.** Filed by W11-03's maker as an explicit HANDOFF (this file sits outside that ticket's `agent-session/**` scope): the existing subset checks only catch a session under-reporting (claiming less than it really touched); nothing caught a session that committed MORE than it declared while its manifest stayed an honest subset of that overreach. Asymmetric already for the gateway-backed path (`refuseIfSessionExceededScope`, W11-03) but the loop package's escape-hatch `createChildProcessSpawn` — D-023's shell-out-to-an-external-CLI runner — had no equivalent guard and relied on this gate's check alone. **Fix**: a symmetric `checkWriteScope(committedFiles, ticket.writeScope, worktree.path)` call — the same `@dokima/git` function (and its internal `HARD_EXCLUSIONS`) both existing checks already use, no third scope matcher. **Deliberately scoped to `committedFiles` (base..HEAD), not the raw untracked working-tree diff**: by the time this check runs, `reRunVerify` and (later) the required validator pack have already executed arbitrary commands with `cwd=worktree.path` and can leave uncommitted side-effect artifacts (verified empirically — `content/validators/_lib.sh`'s telemetry hook writes `docs/work/telemetry.jsonl` into the worktree on every validator run); checking the untracked diff would fail the gate closed permanently on the gate's OWN execution rather than on anything the session did, confirmed by a red fixture calling the fixture's own `.gitignore`-less repo through two `runCloseGate` calls before narrowing the fix. **RED FIXTURES**: an honest manifest whose real commits still contain a path outside `write_scope` is refused; a `.github/workflows/**` HARD_EXCLUSIONS shape is refused even though never claimed; a regression test confirms an untracked, never-committed out-of-scope file does NOT block close. Existing asymmetric checks untouched and verified to not fire on the new fixtures. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing unrelated warning), typecheck clean workspace-wide, 434 files / 3396 tests passed | 1 skipped (+3 new tests), 68 e2e passed.
2026-08-06 W11-14 done — **verify's redaction was pattern-only, and closing that gap surfaced that `read`/`search` had no redaction pass AT ALL — fixed both, not just the one named in the ticket.** W11-11 shipped `redactString` over `verifyTool`'s output but left a KNOWN LIMITATION at `git-tools.ts:17-23`: no `secretValues` were threaded through, so only known-shape credentials (`SECRET_PATTERNS`) were caught — a vault-registered secret with no recognized shape (a bespoke token, a rotated key, a plain password) still reached the routed model. **Fix**: `AgentSessionToolContext` (`tool-executor.ts`) and `GatewaySpawnSessionOptions` (`gateway-session.ts`) gain an optional `secretValues: readonly string[]`, mirroring the two existing precedents for this exact shape (`RenderHandoffOptions.secretValues` in `packages/loop/src/handoff.ts`, `AppendEventOptions.secretValues` in `packages/events/src/append.ts`) rather than threading a live vault handle through the tool context — the caller collects `collectSecretValues(vault, projectDir)` once (async) and passes the array down; omitted, callers still get pattern-only redaction, never a crash. `gateway-session.ts` supplies it into `toolCtx` at construction; `verifyTool`/`redactAndTruncate` pass it to `redactString` alongside `SECRET_PATTERNS`, preserving W11-11's load-bearing order (redact the FULL text before the `MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS` truncation, so a secret can't leak half-matched across the cut). **Acceptance 6 asked whether the same gap exists on any other tool result, and it did — `read`/`search` (`fs-tools.ts`/`fs-search.ts`) returned raw file content/matched lines with NO redaction at all, not even pattern-based.** Those two files are outside this ticket's `write_scope`, but the fix doesn't need to touch them: `tool-executor.ts`'s `createAgentSessionToolExecutor` IS in scope and is the single dispatch point every tool result already passes through, so it now wraps every outcome in `redactDeep(outcome.result, ctx.secretValues ?? [])` before returning — closing the gap for `read`/`search` (and, for free, `list`/`commit`) rather than filing another HANDOFF for a fix that was reachable in scope. Applying `redactDeep` to `verify`'s already-redacted-and-truncated result a second time is a safe, idempotent no-op (already-replaced text matches no further pattern or value), so this doesn't reopen the ordering constraint. **Also fixed the stale comment the ticket's own notes flagged**: `gateway-session.ts`'s KNOWN LIMITATION header claimed the tool-role/`ChatMessage` gap was "filed as a HANDOFF in this ticket's notes" — false; that HANDOFF is W11-13's `runCloseGate` gap, and the tool-role gap is tracked separately as its own ticket, W11-12 (confirmed still `todo`, so the rest of that paragraph's technical description stays accurate and was left alone). **CAUGHT MID-SESSION, not shipped**: an advisor review flagged that the first pass here forgot the claim commit (plan.json still read `todo` with uncommitted work in the tree) and that the acceptance-6 disclosure stopped at "out of write_scope" without checking whether the fix was actually reachable from a file this ticket does own — both corrected before close. **RED FIXTURES**: a plain-word vault secret matching no `SECRET_PATTERNS` shape now redacts through `verifyTool` (`redacted: true`); an unregistered plain word is left alone (negative control against over-redaction); the same plain-word secret sitting in a file the session `read`s through the closed tool set also redacts, exercised end to end through `createGatewaySpawnSession` in `gateway-session.test.ts`. 2026-08-06 W11-19 done — **`writeTool`/`editTool` re-check HARD_EXCLUSIONS and write_scope against the path a write actually lands on, not just the surface path the model supplied.** RAISED CRITICAL by the wave-11 security pass: `refuseIfHardExcluded`/`refuseIfOutsideScope` (`fs-tools.ts`) both ran only against the surface `relPath`, then `fs.writeFile`/`fs.readFile`/`fs.writeFile` ran against `resolveOrRefusal`'s `resolved.abs` — the OS follows any ancestor symlink transparently when that write lands, so a symlink whose surface path is inside write_scope but resolves into `.git` (e.g. `packages/foo/link -> ../../.git`) passed every gate: not hard-excluded on the surface text, passed containment (`.git` IS legitimately inside the worktree, so `assertRealWithinWorktree`'s realpath check has nothing to object to), passed the surface scope check, then physically wrote a git hook that executes on the next commit — and never shows up in `git diff --name-only` at all, so `commitWithScopeCheck`'s commit-time recheck (`packages/git/src/scope.ts`) never gets a chance to catch it either, same as it can't retroactively catch a write that already landed outside the worktree. **DELIVERED**: `assertRealWithinWorktree`/`resolveOrRefusal` (`fs-containment.ts`) now also return `realRelPath` — the fully realpath'd target, worktree-root-relative and posix-separated, computed from the same `real`/`realRoot` the containment check already resolves internally (previously computed then discarded). `writeTool`/`editTool` re-run `refuseIfHardExcluded`/`refuseIfOutsideScope` against `resolved.realRelPath` immediately after `resolveOrRefusal`, keeping the original surface-path checks first for their friendlier ordinary-case refusal message (costs nothing, catches the common case before any filesystem call). **ACCEPTANCE 4, write_scope gets the same treatment**: concluded yes — `refuseIfOutsideScope` has the identical shape (surface-path-only match), so a symlink inside scope resolving to another in-worktree path outside scope is the same defect, just a lesser breach than reaching `.git`; both write and edit now recheck scope against `realRelPath` too, not only hard-exclusions. **RED FIXTURES (acceptance 5)**, planted via `fs.symlink` (never a shell-out), confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code (temporarily reverted `fs-tools.ts`/`fs-containment.ts`, reran, restored): `link -> ../../.git` inside a `packages/foo/` directory with `packages/foo/link/**` in write_scope — write refuses rather than creating `.git/hooks/pre-commit`; edit refuses rather than tampering with a pre-existing one. A third fixture covers acceptance 4's conclusion: a symlink inside scope resolving to a sibling in-worktree directory outside scope is refused by the resolved-path scope recheck. `fs-containment.ts` has no test file of its own (per the ticket's scope pre-check) — all three land in `fs-tools.test.ts`, which already covers it. Gate: lint 0 errors (1 pre-existing unrelated warning), typecheck clean workspace-wide, 437 files / 3432 tests passed | 1 skipped (+3 new), 68 e2e passed. validate-plan/validate-traceability both OK.
2026-08-07 W11-20 done — **`agentRunner.command` (the binary `resolveAgentRunner` spawns on every subsequent build run) now has a per-key gate on the generic settings PUT, matching the shape `copilotEnabled` already had and this key didn't.** `refuseUnconfirmedAgentRunner` (scope-routes.ts) refuses a PUT that CHANGES `agentRunner` to `kind: "external"` unless the same request also carries `"agentRunnerConfirmed": true` (named error `agent-runner-confirmation-required`) — checked against the currently-stored value, not bare presence of `kind: "external"`, so a caller that re-PUTs the value already stored (e.g. `AgentRunnerPanel.tsx` itself, which fetches the full settings map and spreads it back on every save) isn't asked to reconfirm a choice it isn't making. Chose a same-request flag over blocking the key outright (as `copilotEnabled` is): `external` is a legitimate, supported choice (W11-04) with no dedicated route of its own, so a flat block would make a deliberate operator choice indistinguishable from an unattended write (acceptance 4), and a new consent-ledger endpoint would be the "new mechanism" acceptance 3 said not to reach for. **Threat model stated, not left open**: this surface is bearer-token-gated and typically loopback-only, so the realistic unattended writer is a local process or replayed request, not a remote attacker with the token — proportionate to a same-request flag, not a ledgered ack. `AgentRunnerPanel.tsx` already showed `EXTERNAL_AGENT_WARNING` before external can be typed in; it now sends the confirmation flag alongside the value on save, and the server strips the flag before persisting so it never becomes a stored setting. **`parseAgentRunnerSetting` constrained (acceptance 3)**: a `command` past a 4096-char cap or containing a shell metacharacter (`;&|`$()<>\r\n*?~#!'"`, backslash deliberately excluded for Windows paths) now normalizes to the existing MISCONFIGURED empty-command case (W11-18) rather than passing through — chose a metacharacter blocklist over an operator allowlist since `external` is explicitly the escape hatch for "an agent CLI the operator already trusts," no fixed set to enumerate. Read-time only (the parser, not the write path) since `run-build.ts`'s `resolveAgentRunner` is the actual point of spawn and the generic PUT/GET surface never ran the value through this parser before. **RED FIXTURES**: an unconfirmed external PUT refused through the real route (project + global scope, plus a mixed-body variant); the same value re-PUT unchanged succeeds with no flag; a *different* external command with no flag is still refused even after one external command was already confirmed; reverting to `built-in` is never gated; `parseAgentRunnerSetting` rejects 8 shell-metacharacter shapes and an oversized command, and still round-trips an ordinary multi-arg command unchanged (W11-04 regression guard). Advisor review caught the initial gate design keying off bare `kind: "external"` presence rather than an actual change — fixed before close (diffs against `getProjectSettings`/`getGlobalSettings`, read only on the rare confirmation-needed path). The revert to `built-in` is asserted too, covering the never-gated half. **RED FIXTURE, demonstrated both ways rather than asserted**: renamed the server side completely and consistently (the `const` plus all three literals in its own `settings-routes.test.ts`), exactly as a tidy server-side refactor would — `pnpm test` stayed **fully green at 437 files / 3444 passed | 1 skipped**, while this e2e case went **red with `Expected: 200, Received: 403`**, the confirm gate refusing the web client's own PUT.
