2026-08-18 **W13 — the design wave, opened and closed in one session.** The founder's report was not "it looks dated", it was **"I'm stopping every second because it doesn't make sense"** — a comprehension failure, not an aesthetic one, which set the order of work: flow first, palette second. Direction chosen from three options: **instrument panel** (dense, precise, deep slate, one signal accent, mono numerics, state encoded in form), over an assay-office treatment and a quiet-workshop one. **THE ROOT CAUSE WAS STRUCTURAL AND IS WORTH KEEPING**: `apps/web` had 42 passing style guards and an unusable UI, and those facts are not in tension — the guards enforce CONSISTENCY and cannot express QUALITY. They can say "you used the correct grey"; they can never say "this screen has no composition". Underneath that, **Phase 3.5 never ran**: the SDLC this repo ships defines a Design Loop, and `docs/design/` held UX_SPEC, VOCABULARY, contracts and policies with no `tokens.json` — the values in `styles.css` were written as guardrails, never as a system. Every defect was found by driving the running app, not by reading code.

2026-08-18 W13-01 done — **the header stayed put and the nav stopped moving.** Proven by accident: driving the browser, a click aimed at the header landed in a textarea, because on the nine-question Describe form it had scrolled away and there was no route anywhere without scrolling back. The nav also changed shape between views — opening anything replaced the whole thing with a single `← Back`, so the map a user had just learned vanished the moment they acted on it. The destination set is identical everywhere now and the current one is MARKED rather than removed. **The first draft introduced a new hole and the e2e suite caught it**: making the main surface project-only stranded anyone who opened the Roster with no project — the nav offered Roster, they were on Roster, nothing led home. Three specs failed. One spec got SIMPLER rather than different: `decisions-resume` had to click Back before opening Decisions purely because opening a view nuked the nav, and that detour was itself evidence of the defect.

2026-08-18 W13-02 done — **the Describe form stopped trapping people, and the trap was worse than filed.** `title` was seeded from `projectName`, which for an unnamed project is the literal string "Untitled" — and the ready check only required a non-empty title, which "Untitled" SATISFIES. The button was disabled purely for want of an answer while the message read "Answer at least one question and give it a title", blaming a title the user was never asked for and the form had silently supplied. The message now names the ONE precondition actually unmet. **"Build the board" had no class at all** — a bare `<button>` inheriting the generic pill, so it never read as the screen's action even when enabled. Eight `Drafts: Vision` / `Drafts: User personas` captions removed, but the `drafts` FIELD stays: it is the heading `buildInterviewSession` writes into the generated document, so it was always internal and correctly used there — only its doc comment was stale. One fixture of mine asserted nine questions from counting a screenshot; there are eight, and it now asserts against the real constant.

2026-08-18 W13-03 done — **a design system, where there was only a guardrail.** **EVERY RATIO COMPUTED, NOT ASSERTED, and it caught a defect before it shipped**: white on the new dark accent measures **2.46:1** and fails, so `--sw-on-accent` is the ground at 7.56:1 — the identical trap the previous dark accent had, now with its own guard so it cannot return a third time. A second measurement changed a value: the hairline is 1.34:1, fine as decoration and too weak for a structural divider, so `--sw-border-strong` lands at 3.23:1 for WCAG 1.4.11. **Two things the old system simply lacked**: no `--sw-surface` at all, so cards and panes had nothing to sit ON and the app was one flat sheet with lines drawn on it; and a type scale running 0.65–1.25rem, so every text on a Fleet card lived inside about four pixels and nothing could read as more important than anything else. `tokens.json` is GENERATED from the stylesheet with a no-drift guard. No screen re-skinned, deliberately.

2026-08-18 W13-04 done — **a component inventory, measured rather than imagined**: 51 places re-declare the same card border across 14 stylesheets, 8 competing empty-state classes across 7 files, all 15 files reaching for semantic state tokens with no shared component. **The sharpest finding is `.empty-state`** — it ALREADY EXISTED and seven surfaces ignored it and wrote their own. That is the whole problem in miniature: a shared thing existing is not enough, it has to be the path of least resistance. My first count said 49; a shell glob of `*/*.css` never reaches `board/drawer/` two levels down, and the guard that walks the tree found 51. State is decided once, each token keeping exactly one meaning, so the accent never has to mean "good" and a gate result stays readable at a glance.

2026-08-18 W13-05 done — **a project with work waiting stopped looking like five without.** Six cards all read "Ready 0 · Blocked 0 · Done 0", and the one with Ready 1 was visually identical. **The sort was already built and already wired** — `sortByAttention` matches UX_SPEC §2 and `FleetHome` calls it; checked, because this board has found eleven mechanisms with no caller. The gap was purely that nothing VISUAL expressed what the sort already knew. **Three levels, not two**, which the acceptance did not anticipate: "a decision is waiting" and "there is work to do" are different claims on a person, so only the first takes the attention stripe. Two fixes the inventory made obvious: the decide count used `--sw-danger`, saying "something is wrong" when the truth is "a person is needed"; and `dt` was set to 60% opacity against a `dd` at the same size, which is precisely why label and number read at equal weight.

2026-08-18 W13-06 done — **the board stopped spending 85% of itself on nothing.** `auto-fit` + `1fr` splits a row evenly regardless of content, so five empty lanes took the same width as the one holding a card, and the ticket sat in ~130px with its title over three lines. Flex sizes to content. **`--sw-board-column-min` is untouched** — W10-32 raised it on a hunch and W12-30 had to correct that, so the empty case got its own value rather than re-litigating a measured constant. **board.css recorded a constraint I nearly broke**: empty columns deliberately recede rather than vanish, because they are still drop targets and disappearing ones would move the rest under the cursor mid-drag — so this changed their WIDTH, never their presence, with a fixture pinning that. My first value was half a rem short and "In Progress" wrapped; measured on screen, corrected to 6.25rem.

2026-08-18 W13-07 done — **the captured frames finally get compared, and the walker was already broken.** 21 screenshots on every run for waves, and nothing ever looked at them; worse, the walker had not RUN since W12-36 renamed "New Product", and W12-41 broke it again — a capture script nothing runs stops working and says nothing. **No new dependency**: Playwright emits 8-bit truecolour non-interlaced PNG (read from a real IHDR), decoded with `node:zlib` and the five PNG filters in ~60 lines, and the decoder was verified against a known pixel first — the button read `[46,107,255]`, exactly the old `#2e6bff` token. **Threshold measured, not guessed**: comparing two existing runs gave 1–3.8%, but that was real UI change (captured 16 minutes apart during active work); two captures of IDENTICAL code gave the true floor — 16 of 21 frames pixel-identical, worst 0.21%, all timestamps and generated ids. 0.5% is ~2.4× that. An intended change is accepted BY NAME with `--ticket` and `--reason`, recorded beside the baseline; accepting without both is refused. Gate on Node 22: **3669 passed | 1 skipped across 449 files**, **e2e 69 passed**, 56 style guards, 21 frames at baseline.

2026-08-18 **THE FIRST SUPERVISED RUN — performed, failed, fixed, and passed.** W11 exit 2 is demonstrated: a native `SpawnSession` completed a real ticket end to end on a real model, manifest parsed, close gate accepted, receipt minted (`secrets-scan` + `validate-remote-parity` both exit 0), work committed on its own branch with verify printing OK, and the ticket stopped at **`in_review`** — not `done`. That last detail is the maker≠verifier construction holding, and it was the thing most worth watching. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT FAILED, AND THAT IS THE VALUE OF THE EXERCISE**: every fixture-tested part worked — worktree isolation, the MCP allowlist, the tool loop, SC-17/SC-01, the commit path — and it broke on the one thing no fixture could catch. The agent wrote `subtract(a,b)` correctly, verified it to exit 0 and committed it, then reported in prose; `parseCompletionManifest` requires strict JSON with five named fields and the model had been told only "RETURN: Completion Manifest (files produced, verify result, evidence)". **There was also no system message at all** — every instruction it ever received was the rendered handoff. Fixed as **W13-09**: the handoff now carries the schema and one example filled with that ticket's own id and verify command, and the parser was deliberately NOT loosened (accepting prose would mean accepting an unverifiable claim, which is the opposite of what a manifest is for).

2026-08-18 W13-10 done — **a local endpoint can be told how to behave.** Live testing against a remote LM Studio found `prism-ml/bonsai-27b` is a reasoning model spending ~200 tokens thinking before every answer, and the only thing that stops it is `reasoning_effort` — measured across four candidates, where `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking` and a `/no_think` suffix both did nothing. **The adapter could not send it**: `OaiCompatProvider.chat` built a fixed body with no passthrough, so a user configuring a local reasoning model through the GUI had no way to configure the one option that model needs — which cuts against D-024 option (a), since local endpoints are exactly where per-model quirks live. **MERGED FIRST SO THE DERIVED FIELDS WIN**, with its own fixture: a provider entry able to overwrite `model` or `messages` would silently defeat the model matrix and, with it, the maker≠verifier separation routing enforces. Threaded through all five hops (wire → registry → target → config → adapter) because `validate.ts` already warns in its own words that validating a field and dropping it downstream ships something "settable and inert".

2026-08-18 W13-11 done — **the tool-turn cap is configurable, and the cap was not the cause.** `maxIterations` was a session option nothing in `apps/server` ever set, so 12 was every model's value and unreachable without editing source. **THE EXPERIMENT THAT MOTIVATED IT FAILED, which is the more useful result**: the hypothesis was that bonsai parked because twelve turns were too few — with thinking off it produced correct work and used exactly twelve. Given thirty it used twenty across two attempts, produced correct work again, and still emitted no manifest. The cap was never the binding constraint. **The setting was verified to take effect rather than inferred from a null result** — set to 2, the same run went from 226s to 15s; without that check, "the cap is not the problem" and "my setting never reached the session" are opposite conclusions with identical symptoms. Refuses above a ceiling rather than clamping, because a setting accepting any value re-opens T-27 by configuration.

2026-08-18 **FIRST MODEL-FITNESS RESULT.** `qwen/qwen3-coder-next` (local): landed in **one attempt, four tool calls**. `prism-ml/bonsai-27b` (remote LAN): could not close under any configuration tried — with reasoning on it used `write` and destroyed an unrelated `add()` function, then burned nine verify calls flailing; with reasoning off it produced correct code in 88s instead of 136s and still never emitted a manifest, at 12 turns or at 30. **The gates refused all of it**: no manifest → no receipt → nothing merged → ticket parked with evidence naming both attempts, and `main` left correctly red. A weak model produced broken work and the trust boundary held, which is the thesis under an adverse case rather than a happy path.

2026-08-19 W13-19 done — **law 9(a) is enforced, not remembered.** W13-18's own route tests reached a real LM Studio for a day: `resolveModelTarget` falls back to `envTarget`, whose default is `127.0.0.1:1234`, so the tests called whatever model was loaded. They passed when written and timed out a day later on a slower one — a suite reporting the machine's state as if it were the code's. `vitest.network-guard.ts` refuses any non-loopback host and the two local model daemon ports. **The first cut refused every un-stubbed fetch and turned 52 tests red**: those tests boot a Fastify app on an OS-assigned port and talk to it over loopback, which is hermetic — refusing them enforced "no sockets", a different and wrong rule.

2026-08-19 W12-40 done — **a 202 that cannot be honoured is a lie.** W12-20's Start-run button posted, got 202, and the job then found `DOKIMA_SIGNING_KEY` unset and declined — a refusal reaching the caller only through a status poll. Now a 409 at request time with rule `signing-key-unset`. Checked both ends rather than assumed: `BoardView` already renders `problem.detail`, so no web change was needed or made.

2026-08-19 W12-42 done — **the directory picker, bounded on purpose (T-28, SC-19).** Decided **bounded roots** — home, workspace root, parents of registered projects — and recorded what was rejected: unbounded listing, on the argument that the core already opens any path a user names. That is wrong in one way — naming a path requires knowing it, enumeration DISCOVERS paths, so a leaked token would gain a filesystem map. Containment is checked on `realpath`, and a planted symlink out of an allowed root is refused. **`realpath` caught two real bugs beyond the symlink**: on macOS `/tmp` links to `/private/tmp`, so a missing child of a root resolved outside it and reported "outside your allowed roots" — the wrong refusal, the one that sounds like a security problem — while registered projects never matched their own listings.

2026-08-19 W13-16 done — **the session is observable while it works.** `gateway-session.ts` called `provider.chat()`, which resolves once, at the end; `chatStream` had been complete since W2-09 with no session caller. **The mandatory supervised run earned its place**: every fixture passed while the first real run still spent 15 of 18.7 seconds dark, because the opening turn emitted only `tool_calls` and `ChatStreamDelta` carries only `content`. `session.turn_started` closed what this scope could reach (longest dark gap 15s → 1.5s); the rest became W13-20.

2026-08-19 W12-27 done — **the plugin seam's trust model.** Capability scoping (a plugin gets a context scoped to one provider entry, never `CredentialStore`), ledgered consent in the D-019 shape, and **no signature** — signing would require Dokima to decide which authors it trusts, which is an endorsement of exactly the use Anthropic prohibits and the reason D-026 keeps the exposure with the installer. **The export ratchet overruled me**: I wrote and tested `createPluginContext`/`authorizeWithPlugin`, and it refused them for having no caller. Withheld, specified in `docs/design/PLUGIN_SEAM.md`, filed as W12-44.

2026-08-19 W13-22 done — **the stream idle-abort was never attached to the fetch.** Found by running the same loop on a SECOND model. `streamEvents` built `createIdleAbort` and passed `idle.signal` in the RequestInit; `fetchRaw` spread that init and then wrote `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(...)` AFTER the spread, overwriting it. So W13-15 replaced a duration cap with an idle cap and **the duration cap never left** — invisible on any model fast enough never to run a 300s turn. A mid-stream abort also escaped as a raw `DOMException` that `isProviderError` rejected, so the session never absorbed it and the ticket stayed stranded `in_progress`. **My first red fixture passed with the fix reverted** — the fake `fetch` ignored the signal, so a timeout test whose fake never times out.

2026-08-19 W13-20 done — **a tool-calling turn says what it is doing.** `ChatStreamToolCall`, emitted once per call when the NAME lands, not per argument fragment. Adding a third variant to `ChatStreamEvent` broke four consumers that had assumed two — the type system doing the audit. Measured: coder-next 26/64 producing turns (41%) → 6/6 (100%), five naming the tool (`read, edit, commit, verify, verify`). qwen3.6-35b-a3b was already 100% because it narrates every turn.

2026-08-19 W13-21 done — **the challenger was in the right place already.** `createStubChallengerAnchor` had no caller in source at all. BLUEPRINT §3.5 step 4 asks for an independent verifier model "only where no oracle exists" — a build-run session always has one (the ticket's `verify`, re-run out of process), and where there genuinely is none, the challenger IS wired in `packages/pipeline/src/challenger`. Deleted. **The MEMORY stub was kept after checking**: `anti-jarvis-gap.test.ts` uses it as the inert control condition proving the real anchor recalls something. Root cause was a stale comment promising "wiring lands in W5/W7" — both waves shipped, neither wiring was in any ticket's scope.

2026-08-19 W13-23 done — **a session that remembers.** The real `createMemoryAnchor` had existed since W7 with no production caller, so every session ran with no prior findings and no calibration seed. Three "open decisions" were already settled in code: the handle (`log.db`, W12-09's precedent), local-only users (BM25 with no embeddings, `retrieval.ts` AC-1), and the dependency matrix (inject and compose in `apps/server`, W12-04's seam). **A second anchor changed the control flow**: the old block opened `if (validatorResults.length === 0) return`, right for the tool anchor and exactly wrong for memory, whose value is highest on the first turn.

2026-08-19 W12-43 done — **a key the product can make itself (SC-20).** The signing key is an HMAC secret with no public half, so the product mints one with `randomBytes(32)` and stores it as a credential ref rather than asking a user to invent a secret. **The control that matters is the refusal**: a project that already holds receipts and whose key is absent is refused with nothing written to the store, because a replacement does not fail loudly — it makes every existing receipt fail its MAC. An unreadable store is a third, separately named refusal. **The change created a hazard it now owns**: minting means a path under test writes a credential, and `DOKIMA_NO_KEYCHAIN` was pinned nowhere, so the suite would have written a real key into the developer's login keychain.

2026-08-19 W13-24 done — **spend was metered in memory and thrown away.** Found verifying W11's exit criteria. `CostLedger` is a private in-memory array; nothing wrote `costUsd` to the log or any table, and `projects/stats.ts` returned a hardcoded `0` for the Fleet's spend column while saying so in a comment. Now one `spend.recorded` event per metered call — role, model, ticket, run, tokens, cost, numbers only (law 8). Enforcement was deliberately untouched: the breakers read the LIVE ledger during a run and work.

## 2026-08-19 — W11 exit criteria, re-verified

Criterion 3 read "every call is metered — the run's spend ledger is non-zero
and attributable per role". Before W13-24 it could not be shown for **any**
provider, and not because local models are free: nothing persisted spend.

Measured after the fix, on a real local run (`qwen/qwen3-coder-next`):
**20 `spend.recorded` events survived the run**, `role=coding-agent`,
**43,921 prompt tokens / 953 completion tokens**, `usd=0`.

**The remaining gap is in the criterion's wording, not the product.** "Non-zero"
in USD is unprovable on a local-only install by construction, and law 9(b) says
local-only is a full product rather than a degraded one. Token counts are
non-zero and attribution works; a USD figure needs a metered provider. That
clause should be read as "non-zero on a metered provider" — **flagged for the
founder rather than amended here**, since editing an exit criterion is not a
change an implementer should make quietly.

2026-08-19 **SECURITY FINDING — SC-07 is documented as landed and is not in force (W13-25 filed).** An audit of the unwired-export list found `packages/harbormaster/src/sandbox/` — process and container isolation, complete and tested — with **zero production callers**. Both verify paths, the in-session `verify` tool and the out-of-session close gate, run `node:child_process` `exec` with `cwd` and `timeout` only: **the parent environment is inherited whole and the network is open**. `runValidatorPack` spawns the same way, and `RunValidatorOptions` has no sandbox field. **W6-06 is `done` and its `write_scope` was `packages/harbormaster/src/sandbox/**`** — the module and nothing else — so no ticket ever owned a call site. T-4 and T-21 (poisoned content pack, M×H=H) both cite SC-07 as their mitigation; signing (SC-09) is currently the only barrier. SECURITY_CONTROLS.md and THREAT_MODEL.md were corrected in the same commit as the filing, because a security document asserting an absent mitigation is worse than the gap. **Not fixed in place on purpose**: wiring a sandbox into every verify changes execution semantics for every run, and the unavailable-host question — fail closed, or degrade — has to be decided first. This is the sixth instance of the build-then-wire seam (packer W12-04, code index W12-09, anchors W12-05, memory anchor W13-23, spend W13-24) and the first on a security control.

2026-08-19 **SECURITY FINDING — the autonomy dial is written and never read (W13-26 filed).** The same audit that produced W13-25. `FirstRunWizard` and Settings let a user choose Autonomy and `PUT /projects/{id}/autonomy` persists it; **no run path reads `AutonomyMode`** — not `loop-land`, not `berths`, not `loop-claim`, not the CLI. `resolvePauseAction`, whose own doc calls it "the single enforcement point (SC-10)", has zero production callers, as do `assertLedgerValidForNextClaim` and `assertExecutionAllowed`. **CONSTRAINTS C-5 is currently vacuous rather than merely unenforced**: `isNeverAutoPauseSite` is referenced only inside its own module, so nothing consults the dial or its exemption — the constraint holds only because the feature does nothing. **This is worse than an unwired mechanism**: a user makes an explicit choice about what the product may do unattended, it is stored, and it has no effect. `--breakpoint` (wave boundaries) is a different, wired control and is not a substitute. Not fixed in place: wiring the dial turns documented auto-defaults on for the first time, which changes what an unattended run may do.
